⚡ Live Update · v32 · 06 July 2026

July 2026 — Protocol Update

The plan left the boardroom and hit the field. TRT is no longer a strategy on paper — it is live in the bloodstream. This section is the operative record of what changed on the ground in late June / July 2026: the syringe went in early, the kitchen pivoted for the arteries, and a new cardio pillar joined the line. Where this update conflicts with older sections (planned August TRT start, <48% gate), this update is the current truth.

🚨 TWO GATES WERE OVERRIDDEN AT LAUNCH: (1) TRT was initiated at hematocrit 50.3% — the bible gate was <48% before first injection. (2) The clinical rationale is thin: calculated free testosterone already sits at ~2× the deficiency threshold (pro-TRT section is SUPERSEDED). The therapy is running with the benefit case half-price and the risk case full-price. This is not the head of the snake to ignore — it is the one live cardiovascular exposure in the whole file.

⚡ LATEST · 15 Aug 2026 — Mounjaro DISCONTINUED (cost)

🔄 DECISION: stopping Mounjaro (tirzepatide) — reason is cost/value, not the protein struggle. Shifting to intermittent fasting + dietary discipline + TRT as the fat-loss engine. A legitimate call (cost is real; the IF/TRT/diet infrastructure is in place) — logged as a reversible experiment, not a permanent identity test. ⚠️ ROUTE THROUGH PRESCRIBER (relevant to glucose monitoring + the new statin).
📉
Expect fat loss to SLOW — not a failure
Mounjaro drove the bulk of the loss (109.8→102 in 6 wks). IF also improves insulin sensitivity (real overlap, works in your favor) but does NOT replicate GLP-1's magnitude. Judge by the waist tape + cravings, not the scale, and calibrate pace expectations downward so a slower rate isn't read as failure.
Slower ≠ stalled
🍬
Cravings = THE watch-item
Mounjaro was suppressing the sugar cravings that repeatedly broke ketosis (worst when the environment was sugar-filled). Removing the chemical backstop puts the load on dietary control: generous MUFA fat + adequate front-loaded protein is now the PRIMARY craving defense — it has to carry more weight. A slip is a fat/fuel problem, not a character failure.
Diet carries craving control now
⏱️
Keep the window WIDE enough for protein
IF and the 200g target collide if the window is too tight — compressing more food into less time makes protein HARDER. Window fits the protein (≈10:00–20:00), not aggressive 16:8/OMAD. Lean on liquid protein (40g scoops) + front-loading. Re-entry to Mounjaro (or a 2.5mg dose) stays available if cravings overwhelm + loss stalls hard — not a one-way door.
Window ≥ protein need

🎯 THE MATH: 0.33ml × 250mg/ml = 82.5mg per shot. Every 2 days = 3.5 shots/week = ~289mg/week vs the charted 167mg/week. Same balle, nearly double the volume of fire. This is not a cadence tweak — it is a dose escalation wearing a schedule's clothing.

Dose correction — 08 July 2026 · pending prescriber sign-off

✅ DECISION: cut from ~289mg/week back toward replacement. New protocol: 0.2ml Androtardyl q48h ≈ 175mg/week — a ~40% reduction that lands within a whisker of the bible's original charted 167mg/week. Steering the ship back onto the course it was drawn for, before the labs forced the correction.
Candidate schedulePer shotWeekly totalCurve shapeVerdict
0.2ml every 2 days (q48h)50mg × 3.5/wk~175mg/weekFlatter — smaller sips, lower peaks, higher troughs✓ CHOSEN
0.3ml every 3 days (q72h)75mg × 2.33/wk~175mg/weekPeakier — bigger bolus spikes every 3rd dayRejected (only wins on fewer needles)
🎯
Why q48h wins — it's a war on peaks
Same ~175mg/week either way — so this is a smoothness question, not a dose one. Peak testosterone drives EPO/hematocrit hardest (not the average), so flatter peaks = a gentler hand on the red-cell throttle from a 50.3% base. Flatter T also means flatter E2 and a steadier energy tide — damping the "sometimes energy, sometimes not" swing. Kept the good part of the schedule (frequency), cut only the excess (volume).
Peak control
⏳
The long-tail caveat
Enanthate's long ester means the body keeps clearing the old ~289 level for ~2 weeks before settling at ~175. So the mid-August hematocrit reads a blend, trending down — a direction, not a final verdict. The dose cut and the blood test are partners, not substitutes.
Read as trend
🖊️ GATING: This is a prescription change — it routes through the prescriber, not around them. Bring them the 289→175 move + reasoning for countersignature and confirmation of the injection schedule. Effective date: ______ (fill on first 0.2ml shot). Does NOT move the mid-August labs off the calendar.

Implementation — the 9-day ampoule cycle · resolves the 1ml geometry problem

🧮 THE PROBLEM: a 1ml Androtardyl ampoule does not divide into 5 × 0.2ml. It divides into 3 × 0.20ml + 1 × 0.30ml. Draw four even shots and you either waste product or overshoot the dose.

✅ THE SOLVE — proportional spacing: give the larger dose a proportionally longer interval. The cadence bends to match the dose instead of fighting it.
ShotVolumeDoseInterval afterRunning days
10.20 ml50 mg48hDay 0 → 2
20.20 ml50 mg48hDay 2 → 4
30.20 ml50 mg48hDay 4 → 6
40.30 ml75 mg72h (1.5× dose → 1.5× interval)Day 6 → 9
CYCLE1.00 ml (zero waste)225 mg9 days25 mg/day = 175 mg/week ✓
✅
Why this is the elegant answer
Because the larger dose (1.5×) receives a proportionally longer interval (1.5×), the average daily delivery never changes. It lands on exactly 175mg/week — the charted line — with zero product wasted. Superior to both alternatives considered: discarding 0.2ml (wasteful) or 4 × 0.23ml (even, but ~201mg/week).
Zero waste · exact dose
📈
The residual peak — taxed, not eliminated
The 75mg shot produces a peak ~50% taller than the 50mg shots. But enanthate's ~4.5-day half-life means every injection lands into a standing depot, never from zero — so the serum swing is heavily damped. The 3-day gap gives the extra load proportionally more time to clear. Peak discipline preserved.
Damped by the depot
⚠️
Two open flags
1. Use 0.30ml exactly — at 0.35ml you drift to ~184mg/week and lose the alignment.

2. Sterility. Androtardyl 1ml is a single-dose glass ampoule — no rubber stopper, no preservative. Four draws across 9 days is a pharmacist + prescriber question, not a dosing one. Ask directly.
Resolve before next cycle
📅 SCHEDULING: the cycle is 9 days, not 7 — injection days rotate through the week. "Mon/Wed/Fri" no longer maps. Write the schedule down or set recurring alarms.

The two-edged blade — at this dose, benefit and risk both amplify

💪
The edge that pays — muscle-sparing
The one benefit that scales with dose. On a Mounjaro-driven aggressive deficit, ~289mg/week hard-defends lean mass — TRT converts fat loss into recomposition rather than letting muscle leave with the fat. This is the genuine upside of running hot.
Real upside
🩸
The edge that cuts you — HDL
As testosterone climbs toward supraphysiological, the androgen load suppresses HDL — and your HDL is already flagged low. The syringe may be quietly undercutting the very lipid campaign the kitchen is fighting. Left hand vs right hand.
Sabotages lipid front
🎯
The edge that cuts you — hematocrit
More testosterone + more frequency = more erythropoietic pressure, from a 50.3% base already 1.7 points off the 52% MACE/VTE line. Peak-heavy dosing drives EPO harder than steady levels — the q48h volume keeps peaks high. Donation + hydration are now non-negotiable.
Cardiovascular exposure

Kitchen pivot — carnivore re-tuned for the arteries

🐟
Red meat OUT → fatty fish IN
Red meat stopped; rotated to salmon / truite / sardines, eggs, avocado, and egg-protein smoothies. This strips the saturated-fat load that drives ApoB (1.99) and swaps in omega-3 (EPA/DHA) and monounsaturated fat. The primary modifiable lever against your atherogenic particle count.
ApoB play
🎯
The target metrics — September recheck
Primary success metric = ApoB / LDL. Lp(a) 99 is the genetic floor — lifestyle-resistant, don't expect it to move. HDL is a marker more than a lever. This diet template runs untouched until the late-September panel judges it.
Data-gated
🥚
Protein = lean-mass insurance
In an aggressive GLP-1 deficit, protein is the material the training-mold shapes. Keep it high (fish + eggs + egg-protein carry it) or the scale surrenders muscle. If ApoB stays stubborn, shift whole eggs → egg whites (yolks carry the saturated fat).
Protect the machinery

Fish rotation — risk diversification, not preference

SpeciesOmega-3 densityContaminant loadRoleFrequency
🐟 SardinesHighest per calorieLowest — small, short-lived, bottom of food chainThe sleeper. Cleanest omega-3 in the rotation.Increase — underused
🐟 Truite (French)Moderate (~½ the fat of salmon)Low — freshwater racewayLean days: protein without the fat billRegular anchor
🐟 Salmon (farmed)Highest per 100gModerate — open sea cage; PCB/dioxin in feedOmega-3 punch. Label Rouge / bio / certified origin.Cap at 3–4 days/wk, ~300g
💧
Truite vs salmon — the mechanism is the water, not the regulator
Common myth: "Norway is lax, France is strict." False. 99% of Norwegian salmon never receive antibiotics; Norway's own 2014 reassessment found farmed salmon with 30% fewer dioxins and 50% less mercury than 8 years prior. Note too: French supermarket salmon is ~70% Norwegian / 25% Scottish — nothing local about it.

The real difference is farming architecture. Salmon = dense open sea cages, and salmon is not sensitive to polluted water, so farms tolerate it. Trout = freshwater flow-through raceways, and trout is physiologically fragile — it cannot be farmed in dirty water. The fish's own biology is the quality-control mechanism.
Premise corrected
⚖️
The trade-off nobody mentions
Trout is half as fatty as farmed salmon. Cleaner — but less omega-3 per 100g. The swap is not free: you trade contaminant load for omega-3 density. Rotate, don't replace. Three species cover each other's weaknesses — the same logic as portfolio diversification.
Rotate ≠ replace
🚫
Why 400g salmon daily is sub-optimal (not unsafe)
Contaminants clear easily at that volume. The real costs: ~50–60g fat (~500–600 kcal) from one food, carrying an omega-6 load from feed vegetable oils, competing with avocado/walnuts/EVOO in the fat budget — while running an aggressive deficit.

And monoculture inherits one contaminant profile with no dilution. The fish is a means (ApoB), not an end. If it stalls the deficit, it has undermined the mission it was recruited for.
Monoculture risk
🧂 SODIUM LEDGER: smoked salmon (saumon fumé) carries 600–1,200mg sodium/100g vs fresh salmon's ~50mg. Egg-white powder adds ~1,320mg/100g. Two salted protein sources in one day = add no salt elsewhere. Sodium drives water retention → blood concentration → a lever you do not want to lean on at Hct 50.3% with an active donation routine. Also: cold-smoked ≠ cooked — Listeria risk; freeze-first −20°C 24h+ rule applies.

Working diet template · v33 — MUFA-forward, lean protein (SUPERSEDES fish-primary plan)

🔄 WHY THE CHANGE: fatty fish daily proved unsustainable (palatability wall). And stripping fat out triggered sugar cravings that broke ketosis. Root cause = fat too low + under-eating, NOT red meat itself. Fix: lean protein + generous MONOUNSATURATED fat. Restores the craving-free satiety of the old meat-and-butter phase — without butter's saturated fat. A diet actually followed beats an optimal one abandoned.
🍗
Protein backbone — lean, low-SFA
Daily: skinless chicken/turkey breast (leanest complete protein, minimal saturated fat, protects ApoB 1.99).

2×/week red meat for iron/zinc/B12/CoQ10 that poultry lacks: beef heart preferred (~1.5g SFA/100g, richest CoQ10 source, lean muscle) over 5% mince (~2–2.5g SFA). 5% mince = practical fallback.

Egg-white powder (Sermand, 32g protein/40g scoop) — liquid backbone, gap-filler. Cap ~2 scoops (sodium).
Lean = ApoB-safe
🫒
Fat = MUFA, added generously
The satiety engine — abundant, not a garnish. Dry lean meat re-triggers cravings; generous MUFA is the fix.

Cook in AVOCADO OIL (high smoke point). Finish RAW with extra-virgin OLIVE OIL (polyphenols degrade under heat).
Avocado fruit raw (in egg-protein shake) — whole-food MUFA + potassium.
Nuts: macadamia (top MUFA + low-carb) / pecan / hazelnut / almond — dry-roasted 150–160°C, no oil. Walnut RAW (omega-3 heat-fragile). Portion 20–30g — calorie grenades in a deficit.
Satiety + craving control
🐟
Omega-3 = the keystone the fats DON'T replace
MUFA ≠ omega-3. Walnut's ALA converts to EPA/DHA at only ~5–10% — a bonus, not a source.

6 fish-oil capsules/day (~3,000mg EPA+DHA), split, with fattiest meal = now the PRIMARY omega-3 supply since fatty fish is out. NON-NEGOTIABLE. Sardines 1–2×/week as whole-food top-up.

The Sept omega-3 index (>8% target) verifies it's landing in tissue.
Don't let it drop with the fish
🧬 THE FAT-QUALITY LOGIC: this is a fat-type strategy, not a fat-quantity one. SFA raises LDL/ApoB (down-regulates LDL receptors) — modest for most, but real at your genetic numbers. MUFA lowers LDL/ApoB + holds HDL + doesn't oxidize → build the diet here. Omega-3 = anti-inflammatory + triglyceride-lowering. Never replace saturated fat with sugar (lateral move); replace it with MUFA (a win). The trap avoided.
📊 SCOREBOARD (judge by these, NOT the scale — TRT water phase makes it lie): (1) Can you stick to it? (2) Did sugar cravings fade? (3) Is the waist trending down (tape, not scale)? Green on all three over 2–3 weeks = success, whatever the scale says.

New pillar — Zone 2 cardio (30 min per gym session)

DialSettingRationale
Duration30 min, appended to each sessionAlongside resistance work, 3–5×/week
Intensity ceilingMAF 180 − 40 = 140 bpmConversational pace — talk, not sing. Nasal-breathing test.
ModalityBike, incline walk, rower, ellipticalLow joint cost, sustainable, recovery-friendly
TimingFasted AM pairs with cold plungeMaximum fat-oxidation window
💧 MECHANISM: Zone 2 expands plasma volume — it grows the fluid side of the river without touching red-cell count. Since hematocrit is a ratio, fattening the denominator dilutes the reading (the "athlete's pseudo-anemia"). DOUBLE-WIN: the same block hits your primary battlefield — ApoB / lipids — head-on. One session, two fronts.
⚠️ THE TRAP: Not all cardio pulls the same way. High-intensity / hypoxic / altitude work drives EPO up and can RAISE red-cell mass. A hard, sweaty session that dehydrates you transiently SPIKES the reading. Zone 2 is the medicine; redlining is the poison. Cardio is a supporting adjunct to hematocrit — NOT the primary lever.

🧬 Primary vs secondary erythrocytosis — the real science
The scalpel that cuts the panic down to size: TRT erythrocytosis is secondary (clean marrow, EPO-driven) — a different animal from primary polycythemia vera (JAK2 clone). The paper often cited to claim "hematocrit is safe" (PMID 30872370) is about Chuvash erythrocytosis and PV — not TRT. Borrowing its reassurance for testosterone is reading the wrong playbook.
Nuance retained
⚠️ But "secondary" ≠ amnesty
The paper's own witness — Chuvash — is itself secondary and clots hard, independent of hematocrit. And the TRT-specific outcome data still light up: a 74-million-patient database showed Hct ≥52% on testosterone raised MACE/VTE (while T without polycythemia was neutral); TRAVERSE flagged Hct ≥54%. The label deflates "50% = doom" — it does not hand you immunity. Manage the threshold; don't ignore the gauge.
Threshold still real

How TRT reshapes the results — the turbocharger layer

ResultTRT's impactTiming / caveat
Body compositionConverts fat loss into recomp. 12-mo delta vs no TRT: −4 to −7kg fat + +3–6kg leanThe flagship. Validated GLP-1 + TRT synergy blunts deficit muscle loss.
Body fat+2.9kg fat loss vs placebo alone; hunts visceral fat first (androgen receptors); ↑ BMR + insulin sensitivityImproves distribution, not just the number.
MoodLift, clarity, motivation, vivid dreamsWeeks 1–2 (you are Day ~8 now). E2-dependent — do NOT crash it.
Energy / performanceEnergy, libido, sleep ↑; training perf +10–15%Weeks 2–4. Crashed E2 = fatigue misread as "not working."
VascularityMore lean mass + less visceral fat + E2-driven NO vasodilationLast dividend — months 9–18, sub-15% then sub-12% BF. E2 must stay intact.
⚖️ THE HONEST OVERLAY: The bible's benefit numbers assume 167mg/week AND a man climbing out of the basement (low-normal T). Your free T is already ~2× the floor, so the incremental mood/energy/libido dividend is smaller than the brochure — you're adding a floor to a building already tall. The muscle-sparing edge scales up with your dose; the mood/energy edge does not. Turbocharger on an engine already running hot.

Bloodwork additions — what to add, and what to defer

🐟
ADD NOW → Omega-3 Index (recurring panels)
What it is: % of EPA+DHA in red blood cell membranes — not plasma. Membranes turn over slowly, so it is a ~120-day average. The HbA1c of omega-3: cannot be gamed by eating salmon the day before the draw.

Scale: <4% high risk · 4–8% intermediate · >8% target. Most Western men: 4–5%.

Its honest role: it does NOT measure ApoB and improving it does NOT directly lower ApoB. It is an independent risk marker (membrane fluidity, inflammatory signalling, triglyceride handling). Omega-3 is a supporting actor sold as a lead — do not confuse it with the campaign.
Audit, not verdict
🎯
Why it earns a slot anyway
1. It audits compliance + sourcing. The whole diet was rebuilt around fatty fish. If you eat 300–400g/day and the index reads 5%, something is wrong with sourcing or absorption — and you want to know before spending a year on it.

2. Cheap information on a draw already scheduled.

Frame: ApoB (+ LDL) judges the red-meat removal. Omega-3 index judges the fish addition. Both worth knowing. Only ApoB is the campaign.
Secondary marker
⏳
DEFER → Mercury / heavy metals (12-month panel)
Not now. Here is why:
• Your rotation (sardines / truite / salmon) is already one of the lowest-mercury fatty-fish sets available. Mercury bioaccumulates in large predatory fish — swordfish, shark, king mackerel, big tuna. None on your plate.
• Mercury accumulates over months to years. At ~7 weeks on this template, a test reads your pre-protocol history, not your current diet.
• Do not let a curiosity dilute a diagnostic. The August draw has a job: hematocrit, ferritin, iron, T, E2, HDL, ApoB. Mercury reads a hypothetical.
12-month watchlist
📌 MERCURY — TRIGGERS THAT PROMOTE IT TO "ORDER NOW": (1) unexplained neurological symptoms — tremor, numbness, cognitive fog not explained by TRT/sleep/deficit · (2) shift toward large predatory fish (tuna steaks, swordfish) · (3) frequent sushi-grade tuna · (4) otherwise → add to the 12-month comprehensive panel (2027), where it costs nothing to include.

Pharmacology options — reference notes for prescriber discussion (NONE self-started)

⚠️ NONE of these are in use or self-started. Reference notes only — every one is a prescriber decision, screened against TRT, hematocrit, omega-3 (mild blood-thinning) and especially nitrates (fatal with PDE5 inhibitors). Recorded so the reasoning is captured for the right conversation.
DrugClass / what it doesRelevance to this fileVerdict
Nebivolol 3rd-gen beta-blocker + nitric-oxide vasodilation. Lowers BP two ways. The file-ideal beta-blocker IF BP is elevated: metabolically neutral (won't worsen HOMA-IR), spares exercise capacity + erectile function, and is weight-neutral — unlike old beta-blockers (atenolol/metoprolol/propranolol) which cause weight gain via metabolic slowdown + insulin resistance. Only if BP confirmed high. Lowers HR → recalibrate the 140bpm Zone 2 ceiling. Treats pressure, not the hematocrit driving it.
Tadalafil PDE5 inhibitor (vasodilator). Long half-life (~17.5h) → daily low-dose possible. NOT a BP treatment (only −few mmHg). Real uses: erectile function, BPH, vascularity/pump (NO-mediated). Emerging — not proven — CV/endothelial signal. NOT a muscle-builder (pump ≠ hypertrophy; evidence weak/absent). For ED/vascularity, not BP. FATAL with nitrates. If ED on TRT, check E2/hematocrit root cause first.
Enclomiphene SERM — blocks estrogen sensing at pituitary → raises own LH/FSH → own testosterone. Opposite mechanism to TRT. The purified "good" isomer of clomiphene (without zuclomiphene's mood/visual side effects). Preserves fertility + testicular function + drives hematocrit far less than TRT. Legit "should I have gone this route?" question given free T was already ~2× threshold. Alternative to / restart from TRT — not a standard add-on.
Clomiphene SERM — mix of enclomiphene (useful) + zuclomiphene (lingering, side-effect-prone). Cheaper, more available than enclomiphene, but zuclomiphene drives mood changes + rare visual disturbances. Enclomiphene is the cleaner choice where available.
🩺 The prerequisite: measure BP first
Nebivolol/tadalafil BP questions are downstream of a number you haven't measured. TRT raises BP via the hematocrit/E2/fluid channel — get a home BP cuff (the one metric a wearable won't cover). The right drug answers a measured problem, not a hypothetical.
Action: buy a cuff
⚖️ TRT vs Enclomiphene — muscle vs safety
TRT builds more muscle (can exceed natural ceiling). BUT your gain is only the increment above an already-high baseline — and that increment is exactly what drives your hematocrit + HDL suppression. Enclomiphene = somewhat less muscle, far less CV cost. The two questions point opposite ways; the numbers decide.
Worth a specialist chat
🚫 What the evidence does NOT support
Tadalafil for heart protection (observational only, confounded — not a preventive drug). Tadalafil for muscle (transient pump, no hypertrophy evidence). Your real CV levers stay: ApoB, lipidologist, statin decision, weight loss, Zone 2.
Don't oversell

Daily baby aspirin — guidance REVERSED · a real risk-benefit calculation, not a default

🔄 THE REVERSAL: the old "everyone takes a baby aspirin for the heart" advice is outdated. Since ~2019 (ASPREE/ARRIVE/ASCEND trials, USPSTF 2022): for primary prevention (no established heart disease — this is you), aspirin is no longer routinely recommended because the bleeding risk (GI + hemorrhagic stroke) offsets the modest clot-prevention benefit. Still clearly beneficial only for secondary prevention (after a heart attack/stroke/stent — not you).
⚖️
Why your case is genuinely borderline
Points TOWARD considering it: you're higher-risk than an average 40yo — Lp(a) 99 is prothrombotic (clot-promoting), the exact mechanism aspirin counters; ApoB 1.99. The 40–59 age band is an individualized decision, not an automatic no.

Points AGAINST (significant): you already manage clot risk via donation + hydration; you take high-dose omega-3 (mild antiplatelet) — stacking aspirin compounds bleeding risk; blood-donation/procedure bleeding.
Real calculation, both ways
🎯
The calcium score decides it
Same "point zero" CAC scan that guides the statin also personalizes aspirin:
• CAC = 0 → very low event risk → bleeding harm wins → don't take it.
• CAC > 100 (esp. >400) → established plaque → benefit may outweigh bleeding → worth considering.

Turns the question from a guess into a decision.
CAC = the instrument
🚫
The move
Do NOT self-start baby aspirin. Add it to the September lipidologist agenda alongside the statin, Lp(a), and calcium score — and flag the omega-3 dose, because the two together change the bleeding math. Decided with the CAC in hand, not from a bottle on the outdated "aspirin is good for the heart" theory.
Lipidologist decides

Cardiologist visit · 12 Aug 2026 — statin STARTED

✅ OUTCOME: cardiologist reviewed the severe lipid numbers and — despite them — said the overall picture is favourable (young, active, losing weight, improving metabolism, no established heart disease). No urgency, so a measured start: strongest statin at the gentlest dose. Prescribed Rosuvastatine BGR (Biogaran) 5mg, evening, recheck in 2 months.
💊
Rosuvastatin 5mg — "start low, strong molecule"
Rosuvastatin = one of the most potent statins, prescribed at the lowest dose (5mg). Deliberate: already ~35–40% LDL drop, minimal side-effect risk (muscle, glucose — relevant to borderline HOMA-IR), room to titrate up. Take evening, daily (box: "SOIR").
Sensible conservative start
🚫
Red yeast rice — NOW PERMANENTLY CANCELLED
On a prescribed statin, red yeast rice (itself a statin) would double the muscle risk. The hold is now a permanent no. Question settled.
Do not take
💪
Muscle-symptom caveat
Training 5×/wk + TRT = normal soreness. If muscle pain appears, don't assume statin, don't self-stop — report at recheck. Red flag = severe pain + dark/cola urine (rare) → call doctor same day.
Report, don't quit
📋 STILL OPEN for the 2-month recheck: (1) response — did ApoB/LDL drop enough on 5mg, or add ezetimibe / raise dose? (2) confirm cardiologist has the TRT context (HDL suppression). (3) calcium score + carotid Doppler (point-zero imaging) — pursue if not yet ordered. (4) lipidologist referral for the Lp(a) 99.

CV drug reference — how each acts, sorted by target

DrugTargetMechanism — how it worksRelevance
Statine (rosuvastatin)LDL / ApoBBlocks HMG-CoA reductase → liver makes less cholesterol → liver compensates by building more LDL receptors ("vacuums") that clear LDL from blood. −30 to 55%.STARTED — 5mg. Reference tool.
ÉzétimibeLDL / ApoBBlocks NPC1L1 in the gut → less cholesterol absorbed from food/bile. Different door from statin → stacks with it (closes the compensatory absorption). No muscle effect. −15-20% alone; IMPROVE-IT showed added event reduction.First add-on if 5mg statin insufficient
PCSK9 inhibitor (Repatha/Praluent)LDL + some Lp(a)Antibody blocks PCSK9, the protein that destroys LDL receptors → receptors get recycled, not destroyed → far more "vacuums" active → LDL −50-60%. Different mechanism from statin → stacks. Only common drug that also lowers Lp(a) ~20-25%.If LDL stays high; relevant to your Lp(a)
Bempedoic acid (Nilemdo)LDLStatin-adjacent pathway, but activates only in liver (not muscle) → option for statin-intolerant.Backup if muscle issues
Pelacarsen / OlpasiranLp(a) onlySilence the gene making Lp(a) → −80%+. The ONLY thing that truly lowers Lp(a).IN TRIALS — not yet available. Watch.
Red yeast riceLDLMonacolin K = lovastatin. A statin in disguise.CANCELLED — never with prescribed statin
Aspirin (low-dose)ClottingAntiplatelet — thins blood. Primary-prevention guidance REVERSED (bleeding risk). Lp(a) prothrombotic = argument for; omega-3 + donation = against.CAC-score-guided; lipidologist decides
NébivololBlood pressure (NOT cholesterol)Beta-blocker: (1) blocks beta-1 → slows heart; (2) releases nitric oxide → dilates arteries. Two levers.Only if BP confirmed high
TadalafilBlood flow (NOT cholesterol)Blocks PDE5 (the enzyme that destroys the NO "dilation signal" cGMP) → dilation lasts longer. Prolongs existing vasodilation.ED / vascularity, not CV treatment

Imaging — "photographing the arteries": hard vs soft plaque

🫀 Coronary calcium score (scanner, no injection)
Measures calcium in coronary arteries (Agatston score) = HARD/calcified plaque only. Your "point zero" baseline. Guides BOTH the statin and aspirin decisions (0 → watchful wait; >100/>400 → treat). Photographs your past — you don't "achieve" a 0, you have one or you don't.
Coronaries · hard plaque
🩻 Carotid Doppler ultrasound
Ultrasound of the neck arteries + intima-media thickness. Sees SOFT plaque (young, cholesterol-rich, unstable — the kind that ruptures → heart attack) that calcium score MISSES. No radiation. Important for you: young + Lp(a) 99 = soft-plaque profile → a calcium score of 0 could be falsely reassuring.
Catches what CAC misses
🧱 Why two plaque types matter
Soft plaque = young, lipid-rich, thin cap, unstable → ruptures → clot → infarct. Invisible to calcium score. Hard plaque = old, calcified, stabilised → what CAC detects. The two scans are complementary: CAC for coronaries, Doppler for early/soft disease.
Soft = the dangerous one

🏋️ Gym Workout

Gym Workout

Machine-based, Heavy-Duty-inspired training for the current phase — deficit + TRT + limited recovery. Organised by muscle group: for each, the optimal exercises, what the muscle does functionally in each movement, and why that short list covers the muscle's full growth potential and fibre spectrum. The progression engine (how to actually get stronger) sits at the end.

📆 THE WEEKLY SPLIT — 5 training days + 1 rest, then repeat

DayWorkoutEmphasis
1Chest + Back + Abs AUpper-body mass · Abs A (crunch + hanging raise)
2LegsLegs
3Arms + Forearms + Cardio + Abs BArms · Forearms #1 (HEAVY) · Abs B (obliques — woodchop)
4Shoulders + Traps + CardioShoulder specialization
5Abs C + Forearms + CardioAbs C (main session) · Forearms #2 (PUMP)
6RESTFull recovery → repeat
Why this sequencing works: Legs (Day 2) sits between Chest+Back and the direct Arm/Shoulder days — so the pressing/pulling muscles get a day off before being hit directly, breaking the old shoulder/elbow "3-days-in-a-row" stack. Cardio spread across the lighter days (3–5). Abs on Days 1/3/5 — a priority, spaced ~48h then rest (crunch/hanging-raise emphasis, obliques moderate; see Abs specialization below). Priority muscles get extra attention without training everything every day.
⚠️ FOREARM SAFEGUARDS (forearms run 2×/wk by choice — medial elbow is the #1 overuse risk on TRT, already loaded by every row/pulldown/chin-up + gripper): (1) Day 5 forearms = strictly LIGHT, high-rep (15–20+) PUMP, never near failure — genuinely different from Day 3's heavy session, not a second heavy hit. (2) BIAS BOTH to the extensors (reverse wrist curls, reverse curls) — grip work already overloads the flexors, and the medial elbow IS a flexor-tendon issue, so more flexion aggravates it; extension work is protective + balancing. (3) ELBOW HAS VETO — any medial-elbow ache/tenderness → the Day 5 pump session is the FIRST thing to cut. (4) Keep the two sessions ≥48h apart; warm up before the heavy one.
📊 RECOVERY GOVERNOR: this is 5 training days + 1 rest, in a DEFICIT, on TRT, during a phase of questioned recovery. Sequencing is good, but recovery TIME is the real limiter — watch for across-the-board strength stalls, worsening sleep, or persistent watery/flat feeling → those signal the need for a 2nd rest day. Don't be too proud to insert one; abs (lowest priority) can always be folded into another day to free recovery.
🧬 FIBRE PRIMER — read once, applies everywhere. Every muscle contains two fibre types: Slow-twitch (Type I) — fatigue-resistant, endurance, recruited FIRST and best grown by higher reps (15–20) + time under tension; and Fast-twitch (Type II) — powerful, fatigue quickly, recruited only as load/effort climbs, best grown by heavier load (6–10 reps). By the size principle, a muscle recruits slow fibres first and adds fast fibres progressively as the set gets heavy or hard. Conclusion: exercises cover the muscle's movements; REP-RANGE CYCLING covers its fibres. That's why every muscle below lists a rep range, and why the 8-week plan at the end alternates heavy and high-rep blocks.

Quadriceps musculature — schematic

Fibre: MIXED

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🦵 QUADRICEPS — 4 muscles, front thigh · function: knee extension (+ hip flexion, rectus femoris)

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Leg Press (rotate w/ Hack Squat)Compound knee extension under heavy load — all 4 heads drive the sled away by straightening the knee against resistance. Loads the quad through its strongest range.2–3 × 10–15
Leg ExtensionPure isolated knee extension — the quad's ONLY job with no help from glutes/hams. Targets the rectus femoris and lets you reach true failure + peak contraction safely.2 × 12–20
Why 2 exercises = full coverage: the quad has exactly one primary function (knee extension) but benefits from both a compound loaded version (Leg Press — heavy, fast-twitch, most growth stimulus) AND an isolated version (Leg Extension — reaches failure safely, hits the rectus femoris which the press under-emphasises). Together they cover heavy compound + isolated peak, and via rep cycling (Press heavy 6–10 / Extension high-rep 15–20) both fibre types. More quad exercises = repeating knee extension = redundancy.

Hamstrings musculature — schematic

Fibre: FAST-LEAN

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🦵 HAMSTRINGS — 3 muscles, back thigh · TWO functions: knee flexion + hip extension

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Leg Curl (seated or lying)Knee flexion — the hamstring bends the knee, curling the heel toward the glute. This is one of the ham's two jobs and the one a squat/press never trains.2–3 × 10–15
Hip ThrustHip extension — the hamstring (with the glute) drives the hips upward. Covers the ham's SECOND function, which the leg curl misses.2–3 × 10–12
Why 2 exercises = full coverage: the hamstring is unique — it's a biarticular muscle with TWO distinct functions (bends the knee AND extends the hip). A leg curl alone trains only half of it. You need one knee-flexion movement (curl) AND one hip-extension movement (hip thrust) to cover the whole muscle. This is why hams need two different-looking exercises where quads can share a theme.

Glute musculature — schematic

Fibre: FAST-LEAN

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🍑 GLUTES — max / medius / minimus · functions: hip extension (max) + hip abduction (med/min)

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Hip ThrustHip extension against load — glute max's primary job, driving the hips from flexed to locked-out. The single best-loaded glute-max movement.2–3 × 10–12
Hip Abduction (optional)Hip abduction — pushing the legs OUTWARD works glute medius/minimus (the "outer/side" glute), a different function the thrust doesn't cover.2 × 15
Coverage note: Hip Thrust fully covers glute max (the big one, hip extension). Add Hip Abduction only if you want the side-glute (medius/min) for shape/hip stability — it's a genuinely separate function (abduction ≠ extension). Note: the abduction machine (legs pushing OUT) is glute, NOT the inner-thigh adductor.

Calf musculature — schematic

Fibre: SLOW-DOMINANT

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🦵 CALVES + the ADDUCTOR gap

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Standing Calf RaiseAnkle plantarflexion (rising onto toes). Standing = knee straight → targets the gastrocnemius. Its partner the soleus is heavily slow-twitch → needs high reps.2–3 × 12–20
Hip Adduction (optional — fills the gap)Hip adduction — squeezing the legs INWARD works the inner-thigh adductors, the one leg muscle the core plan doesn't hit.2 × 15
↳ Variation if machine broken: Wide-&-high-stance Leg Press (or wide Hack Squat)A wide, high, toes-out foot stance recruits the adductors hard as hip extensors — the inner thigh must control + drive the hip out of the wide position. Closest substitute, no extra machine. (Cable adduction = the direct functional alternative if cables are free.)1–2 sets wide-stance
🔬 CALVES ARE THE CLEAREST FIBRE EXAMPLE: the soleus is one of the most slow-twitch-dominant muscles in the body (it holds you upright all day). That's why calves get the highest rep range (12–20) and respond to volume/time-under-tension more than heavy low-rep work. The gastrocnemius has more fast-twitch, so an occasional heavy 8–10 block helps — but calves live in higher reps.

Chest musculature — schematic

Fibre: MIXED (slight fast)

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🫀 CHEST (pectorals) · TWO functions: horizontal press + horizontal adduction

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Chest Press (Machine)Horizontal pressing — the pec drives the arms forward/away from the torso under heavy load. The compound builder, hits the whole chest + front delt + triceps.2–3 × 8–12
Chest Fly (Pec Deck)Horizontal adduction — the pec brings the arms together across the body in an arc. Isolates the chest's "hugging" function with a deep stretch the press can't give.2 × 12–15
Why 2 = full coverage: the pec does two things — press (arms forward) and adduct (arms together). The press builds mass under heavy load (fast-twitch); the fly trains the stretched adduction the press misses and reaches failure safely (great for the squeeze/mid-chest). Five fly variations are all the SAME adduction pattern — one is enough. Press + fly + rep cycling = complete chest, both fibres.
📐 REGIONAL EMPHASIS — cover upper/mid/lower by ANGLE, not by adding exercises. The pec has regions: upper (clavicular), mid, lower (sternal). The angle of the movement shifts the emphasis: incline (bench up ~30°, or low-to-high cable fly) → upper; flat (or mid cable) → mid; decline (or high-to-low cable) → lower.

Option B (the efficient way — angle cycling, mirrors rep cycling): keep ONE press + ONE fly, but rotate the angle every few weeks — e.g. an incline-press block, then a flat-press block; a low-to-high fly block, then a mid block. You cover all regions over time without exploding back to 6 exercises. Priority: favour INCLINE — the upper chest is the most under-developed + visually impactful region. Lower chest rarely needs dedicated work (hit by most pressing).

💡 THE PRINCIPLE (applies to several muscles below): ANGLE covers the muscle's REGIONS; REPS cover its FIBRES. Two different variables, two different jobs — both rotate over time, neither needs piling on exercises.

Back musculature — schematic

Fibre: MIXED (postural = slow)

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🔙 BACK (lats, traps, rhomboids, rear chain) · TWO functions: vertical pull + horizontal pull

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Lat PulldownVertical pull (shoulder adduction) — the lats pull the arms down from overhead toward the torso. Builds back WIDTH. This is the lat's primary function.2–3 × 10–12
Seated Row (cable/machine)Horizontal pull (shoulder extension + scapular retraction) — pulling the handle to the torso works mid-back THICKNESS: rhomboids, mid-traps, lats from a different angle.2–3 × 10–12
Chin/Pull-up (optional)Loaded vertical pull with bodyweight — maximal lat + biceps recruitment.2 × to failure
Why 2 = full coverage: the back pulls in two planes — vertically (pulldown → width, lats) and horizontally (row → thickness, mid-back). Miss either and half the back is undertrained. This is why one pulldown + one row is the minimum complete back — the six rows in the old plan were all the SAME horizontal pull. Two planes + rep cycling = complete back.
📐 GRIP/ANGLE EMPHASIS (Option B — rotate over time): the back's regions shift with grip width + row angle. Pulldown: wide grip → outer lats/width; close/neutral grip → lower lats + more biceps. Row: low/underhand angle → lower lats; high angle (face-pull-height) → upper back/rear delts. Keep one pulldown + one row, but rotate grip and angle every few weeks — wide-grip block then close-grip block; low-row block then high-row block. Covers the whole back over time without adding exercises.

Deltoid musculature — schematic

Fibre: MIXED (side/rear slow-lean)

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🎯 SHOULDERS (deltoids) — 3 HEADS, each a separate function

ExerciseWhat the muscle does here (function)Sets × Reps
Seated Shoulder PressOverhead press — front (anterior) delt drives the arms overhead. Compound, also hits side delt + triceps.2–3 × 8–12
Lateral RaiseShoulder abduction — side (lateral) delt lifts the arm out to the side. THE width-builder; only lateral raises train this head directly.2–3 × 12–15
Rear Delt Fly / Face PullHorizontal abduction / external rotation — rear (posterior) delt pulls the arms backward. Also the posture/joint-health head.2–3 × 12–15
ShrugScapular elevation — upper traps lift the shoulders toward the ears.2–3 × 12–15
Why 3 (+traps) = full coverage: the delt is genuinely THREE separate muscles with three functions — press (front), raise (side), reverse-fly (rear). Each needs its own movement; no single exercise hits all three. Five lateral raises train only the side head five times (redundant); you need one PER head. Front delt is also hit by all chest pressing → skip front raises. Favour the FACE PULL for rear delt = builds the head AND the external rotators that protect the shoulder joint (key on TRT).
📐 NO ANGLE ROTATION NEEDED HERE: unlike chest/back (one muscle, multiple regions by angle), the delt's three "regions" are literally three separate muscles — so your 3 exercises ARE the angle coverage. Press/lateral/rear already cover all three heads directly; adding angle variations would just repeat a head. Same logic as legs (quads/hams/glutes are distinct muscles, not angle-regions of one). Only rotate REPS here for fibres.

💪 ARMS (biceps / triceps / forearms) — elbow-cautious · anatomy + optimized program

Arm musculature — schematic

Fibre: MIXED (biceps/triceps) · forearms slow-lean

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale. Colours group muscles by the exercise that targets them (table below).

Per-exercise → exact muscle worked

ExercisePRIMARY muscleSecondaryHow / why
Curl (supinated)Biceps brachii (both heads)Brachialis, brachioradialis (light)Elbow flexion palm-up — biceps' strongest line + supination
Incline curlBiceps — LONG head (peak/outer)BrachialisArm behind body = stretched long head
Preacher / cable curlBiceps — SHORT head (inner)BrachialisArm in front = shortened, short-head bias; cable = elbow-friendly constant tension
Hammer curlBrachialis + brachioradialisBiceps (light)Neutral grip shifts load off biceps → arm THICKNESS (the visible side muscle)
Reverse curlBrachioradialis + wrist extensorsBiceps (light)Pronated grip — the forearm-mass + elbow-balancing move
Triceps pushdownTriceps lateral + medial headsLong head (light)Elbow extension, arm at side
Overhead ext.Triceps LONG headLateral headArm overhead stretches + loads the long head (pushdown misses it)
Wrist curlWrist/finger FLEXORS (inner forearm)—Wrist flexion
Wrist extensionWrist EXTENSORS (outer forearm)—Wrist extension — protective, balances the flexor-heavy grip work
Gripper / farmer holdFinger flexors + gripWhole forearm (isometric)Crushing/holding grip strength + size
Wrist rollerWrist FLEXORS + EXTENSORS (both)Finger flexors, grip enduranceRolling the weight up works flexors + extensors continuously — a complete forearm burnout, endurance-biased
Pronation/supinationPronator/supinator + brachioradialisBiceps (supination)Forearm rotation — the often-missed function

Optimized program — split across Day 3 (heavy) + Day 5 (pump) for arm/forearm size WITHOUT overloading the elbow

DayBlockExercises
Day 3 — Arms (heavy)BicepsIncline curl 3×8–12 (PRIMARY — peak/long head) · Hammer 3×8–12 (brachialis) · Preacher/standard 2–3×10–15 (short-head base) (do 2/session — incline + hammer default; rotate preacher in occasionally. NOT incline+standard same-day — redundant on elbow)
TricepsCable pushdown 3×8–15 · Overhead cable ext. 3×10–15
ForearmsReverse curl 3×10–15 · Wrist curl 3×12–20 · Wrist extension 3×15–20
Day 5 — Forearms #2 (pump)Grip/rotationGripper 3 sets · Farmer hold 2–3×30–60s · Wrist roller 2–3 sets (up-and-down) · Pronation/supination 2×12–20/side (light/high-rep — never near failure)
Why this = COMPLETE arm coverage: elbow flexion ✓ · supination/biceps ✓ · brachialis ✓ · brachioradialis ✓ (hammer + reverse curl) · all 3 triceps heads ✓ · wrist flexion ✓ · wrist extension ✓ · grip/fingers ✓ · pronation/supination ✓ · grip endurance (wrist roller) ✓. The old plan missed brachioradialis + pronation/supination — reverse curl + rotations fill those. Biceps get an added stretched-position angle (incline) for regional completeness.
🏆 INCLINE vs STANDARD CURL — for BOTH hypertrophy AND aesthetics, INCLINE wins (don't do both same-day — redundant on the elbow): (1) Hypertrophy: incline loads the biceps in the STRETCHED position (arm behind body) → stretch-mediated hypertrophy, more growth per set than the standard curl's mid-range bias (standard loses tension at the bottom). (2) Aesthetics: incline preferentially loads the LONG HEAD = the PEAK (height/roundness + outer sweep) — the head that makes an arm look impressive and the one hardest to grow. The two goals point to the SAME exercise — no trade-off. Caveat: incline is more shoulder-demanding + you'll use less load → keep controlled, don't force heavy into the deep stretch (TRT shoulder/tendon caution).
💪 THE AESTHETIC-ARM FORMULA (for big, impressive arms): Incline curl = PEAK / long head (primary — the money-maker) · Hammer curl = brachialis (sits UNDER the biceps → pushes the peak HIGHER + widens the whole arm — underrated aesthetic weapon) · occasionally rotate Preacher/standard curl = short-head BASE / inner-arm fullness. Peak + thickness + base. Don't run incline in isolation (would under-develop the short-head base), but incline is the centerpiece to prioritize.
🔧 TRICEPS PUSHDOWN — attachment + grip choices: ROPE vs BAR: rope has the slight edge for hypertrophy/aesthetics — hands spread apart at the bottom → fuller peak contraction + better LATERAL-head (width/horseshoe) recruitment, and freer wrists = kinder on the elbow. Bar loads heavier / cleaner progression. Small gap → default ROPE, rotate a BAR block in for heavy overload (same attachment-rotation logic as chest angles). STANDARD (pronated) vs REVERSE (supinated) pushdown: standard wins for mass — heavier load, lateral head (width). Reverse shifts to the MEDIAL head but you can barely load it (weak grip) → optional detail work only. Do NOT swap your overhead extension for a reverse pushdown — overhead hits the LONG head (biggest, most mass-relevant triceps head); trading it for the small medial head is a downgrade. Keep: standard/rope pushdown (lateral) + overhead ext (long head) = the two heads that build the arm.
⚠️ ELBOW (the limiter — this program RAISES arm frequency, so respect it): (1) Rotate the 3rd biceps exercise rather than doing incline+preacher+hammer every session — 2 biceps moves/session is plenty on top of all the indirect pulling. (2) Biceps already worked by Chest+Back rows, triceps by all pressing → this is why arms stay ONE heavy day. (3) Bias the extensors (wrist extension, reverse curl) — grip work overloads the flexors and the medial elbow IS a flexor-tendon issue, so extensor work is protective. (4) Total weekly forearm load is HIGH (Day 3 + Day 5 + indirect grip + home gripper) → elbow has veto: first medial ache, cut Day 5. (5) All direct arm work 0–2 RIR, not to failure.

Core musculature — schematic

Fibre: MIXED (endurance-lean)

Schematic only — approximate positions, not to scale.

🔥 ABS / CORE — PRIORITY specialization (3 exposures/6-day cycle, Days 1/3/5)

🎯 ABS ARE A PRIORITY (alongside shoulders + forearms) → 3 exposures spaced ~48h (Days 1/3/5, then rest). The win is FREQUENCY + PROGRESSIVE OVERLOAD + MOVEMENT DIVERSITY, NOT hundreds of reps. Treat the rectus like any hypertrophy muscle: meaningful load, controlled reps, 0–2 RIR. DON'T repeat the ab wheel all 3 days — cover the core's different jobs across sessions.
DayExerciseSets × RepsFocus / function
Day 1 (after Chest+Back) — Abs ACable Crunch3 × 8–15Spinal flexion — rectus thickness (loadable)
Hanging Leg/Knee Raise3 × 8–15Lower-region — curl the PELVIS up, not just legs
Day 3 (after Arms) — Abs BCable Woodchop (high-to-low)3 × 10–15/sideRotation — oblique hypertrophy (torso rotates, not arms)
Pallof Press (optional)2–3 × 10–15/sideAnti-rotation — stability (secondary to woodchop for size)
Day 5 — Abs C (main session)Ab Wheel3 × 6–12Anti-extension — whole anterior core (ROM > reps)
Cable Crunch3 × 10–15Rectus flexion
Hanging Leg Raise3 × 8–15Rectus + pelvic control
Why this beats ab-wheel×3: the core does 4 jobs — flexion (cable crunch), anti-extension (ab wheel), pelvic curl/lower (hanging raise), rotation (woodchop). Diversity across the 3 sessions = complete coverage. Priority order for YOUR goal: cable crunch > hanging raise > ab wheel > one cable-oblique. Rep sweet spot ~8–15 (ab wheel 6–12) — NOT 3–5 heavy (form breaks) and NOT 30–50 (cardio becomes the limiter). Progressive overload example: cable crunch 60kg ×15/14/12 → clean 15/15/15 → 65kg ×11/10/9 → rebuild.
⚠️ VOLUME + WAIST-TAPER CAVEATS: ~19 direct ab sets/cycle is the HIGH END — don't add daily morning ab-wheel on top; if recovery tightens (deficit!), trim Day 3 first. Keep most sets 1–2 RIR, only the last set near failure. KEEP OBLIQUE WORK MODERATE — bigger obliques THICKEN the waist, working against your broad-shoulder/narrow-waist V-taper goal → emphasise rectus, one moderate oblique movement only. And the eternal truth: abs are UNCOVERED by fat loss, not built by volume — this training thickens them, the deficit reveals them. Both needed; don't let ab volume crowd out recovery.

The progression engine — how to actually get stronger

Few exercises means progression can't come from ADDING exercises — it comes from the variables within each one. The exercise never changes; the stimulus always does. There are two buckets, and they progress the muscle through two different mechanisms:

① VOLUME techniques — progression by TOTAL WORK (sets × reps × load)

LeverHowRole
Reps @ same weightBeat last week's reps before touching load. 10→11→12→…→top of range.PRIMARY — every session
Load (double progression)At top of range (e.g. 15) → +smallest increment (2.5kg) → reps reset to bottom → climb again.After topping the rep range
Why this is the trackable backbone: it's objective — "more reps or more weight than last week?" is a clear yes/no you log every session. That measurable progressive overload is the most established growth driver. But it STALLS in a deficit — you can't keep adding load under-fuelled. That's when bucket ② takes over.

② INTENSIFICATION techniques — progression by STIMULUS-PER-SET (make the same work harder)

LeverHowRole
Tempo / eccentricSame weight, 3–4s lowering phase. More tension, no added load.Tendon-safe — FAVOR on TRT
Rest-pauseNear-failure → rest 10–15s → 3–5 more → repeat. Extends the set past failure, no spotter.Plateau-breaker, machines
Reduced restCut rest 2min → 90s → 60s. Density progression.Occasional
Drop setsFailure → −25% → failure → −25% again.Sparingly — costs recovery you lack in a cut
Why these matter MORE for you than usual: they extract more stimulus from the SAME weight — so they're how you keep progressing when volume can't move (constant in a deficit). A slow eccentric makes 10 reps at 50kg far harder than fast 10 reps at 50kg — same volume on paper, more stimulus in reality. Harder to track (that's why volume stays the backbone), but per-set they can drive as much growth as adding a rep — AND they're joint-friendlier than piling on load (fits your elbow/shoulder caution). In your cut, you'll lean on these heavily — not as second-string, but because pure load progression is scarce.
⚠️ THE DISCIPLINE (spans both buckets): change ONE variable at a time — whether pushing volume (reps OR load, not both) or adding intensification (tempo OR rest-pause, not both) — so you can read what's working. 2–3 working sets per exercise. Volume = the default when you can add it; intensification = what you reach for when you can't (often, in a deficit).
📅 THE 8-WEEK FIBRE PERIODISATION (this is how you recruit ALL fibres over time): Weeks 1–4 — double progression in the moderate range (10–15), reps then load. Weeks 5–8 — a HEAVY block (6–10 reps), chasing load with fast-twitch emphasis. Then loop back. Keep slow-twitch-dominant muscles (calves/soleus, side & rear delts, abs) biased to higher reps (12–20) throughout. Cycling the ranges is what covers the full fibre spectrum the single exercises otherwise wouldn't.

Optimal rep cycling per muscle — cycle for all, bias for the clear cases

MuscleFibre leanHeavy block (wk 5–8)Moderate block (wk 1–4)Notes
Quads (Leg Press)Mixed6–1010–15Big supported compound → safe to go genuinely heavy
Quads (Leg Extension)Mixed10–1215–20Isolation on knee joint → keep a touch higher than the press
HamstringsFast-lean6–1010–15Tolerate + benefit from heavy; hip thrust can go heavy, leg curl moderate

How to read this: the RULE is cycle every muscle between its two ranges over the 8 weeks (covers all fibres, since every muscle is mixed + you can't know your personal ratios). The BIAS is a light thumb on the scale: calves + forearms + rear delt stay high (real slow-twitch dominance), elbow/shoulder movements bias moderate (tendon safety on TRT — get intensity from tempo/rest-pause, not maximal load), and big supported compounds (leg press, chest press, back, glutes/hams) get the genuine heavy block where fast-twitch growth lives. Cycling is the law; fibre bias is the fine-tune.

📊 DEFICIT REALITY (Mounjaro stopped, still cutting on TRT): progression is slow + non-linear. Most weeks = +1 rep, a slower eccentric, or HOLDING numbers while fat drops — that's a WIN (stronger per bodyweight, muscle preserved through a cut). Bias tension-levers (reps, tempo, rest-pause) over load-chasing: safer for tendons + the deficit caps strength gains anyway. Real linear load progression returns at maintenance/surplus.
🔗 CROSS-DAY OVERLAP: biceps (rows), triceps (press), front delts (all pressing), traps (rows), core (everywhere) get indirect work across days → keep dedicated arm & shoulder work to ONCE/week, and treat the medial-elbow + shoulder as the limiting tendons (stop shy of failure on direct arm/pressing work).

Everything nutrition in one place — the current daily baseline first (what to actually eat today), then the systems behind it: the MUFA-forward template, the carnivore/Mounjaro food logic, the fasting protocol, and the protein-distribution science. The baseline leads because it's the practical answer; the rest is the reasoning that supports it.

🥤 Protein Distribution — The Real Science

200g protein in one shake vs spread across meals — this is one of the most important practical decisions for muscle preservation during your transformation.

⭐ CURRENT REALITY (Aug 2026 — Mounjaro + TRT + one post-gym eating window): the constraint is can only eat AFTER the gym, no pre-gym food, gym time floats (midday–6pm) so the window sometimes only opens ~20:00. This supersedes the multi-meal distribution theory below — that theory is correct in a lab but assumes a window you don't have. Here's what actually works.
🥤
SHAKE FIRST, then chicken — the order that beats appetite
Mounjaro suppresses appetite + slows gastric emptying → solid chicken fills you fast. Liquid slides past that wall. Lead with the big concentrated shake (3× 40g scoops ≈ 94g in a little water — egg powder is 0g fat / 2.3g carb / 78g protein per 100g, ideal), THEN chicken as appetite allows, THEN an optional small second shake to top up. Protein-first, always — every bit of stomach space is precious in one window. Banking 94g from one drink means even a bad-appetite night starts from a strong floor.
Shake → chicken → top-up shake
🔬
The "30g absorption cap" is a MYTH
You absorb and use essentially ALL the protein you eat. What plateaus is the peak synthesis rate of a single meal (~30–50g), NOT total absorption — the excess is used more slowly + for other functions, NOT wasted/flushed. So 150g in a 3-hour window hits the target and counts. For muscle preservation in a deficit, total daily protein dominates — perfect distribution is a minor factor. A real 150g in your window beats a theoretical 4-meal split you can't execute. Evening protein also releases amino acids slowly overnight → feeds recovery through sleep.
Total daily > distribution
🎯
Target reset: ~150g (not 200g)
At 102kg → 75kg target, muscle preservation needs ~1.6g/kg of TARGET weight ≈ 120–150g. So 150g isn't a compromise — it's genuinely enough, and far more reachable in one window. One 3-scoop shake (94g) + one chicken breast (~50g) = ~145g in two items. Spread across the 3-hour window (shake 20:00 → chicken 21:00 → shake 22:00) to ease the fullness wall — which also captures the small distribution benefit for free. Consistent 150g beats a stressful, missed 200g.
150g consistent = the win
📌 NOTE: the detailed multi-meal MPS tables below are the theoretical ideal for a WIDE eating window (kept for reference / future maintenance phase). They do NOT fit the current one-post-gym-window constraint — don't let "one shake wastes protein" (an overstatement of the per-meal synthesis ceiling) discourage the shake-first approach. In your setup, the shake is the tool that makes the target reachable at all.
❌
One 200g shake — why it fails
Your body CAN absorb all 200g — absorption is not the issue. The problem is muscle protein synthesis (MPS).

MPS has a per-meal ceiling of approximately 0.4–0.55g per kg bodyweight — at 109kg this means ~44–60g optimally utilized per meal.

The remaining 130–160g gets oxidized for energy or converted to urea — it does NOT build more muscle, does NOT store as protein reserves.

One shake = 1 MPS trigger. You are wasting 65–70% of expensive beef protein isolate.
Avoid — massive waste
✅
3–4 meals — optimal MPS triggers
Each protein meal above the leucine threshold (~2.5–3g leucine) triggers a full MPS cycle.

3 meals × 65g = 3 MPS cycles in your 6h window
4 meals × 50g = 4 MPS cycles in 8h window (16:8)

Beef protein is slow-digesting — releases amino acids over 4–6 hours. A meal at 12:00 and 15:00 gives amino acid availability spanning nearly the whole window.

4 MPS triggers vs 1 = dramatically more muscle building signal over 24 hours.
Optimal strategy
🔬
New research update — 100g meals
A 2023 study found that 100g protein in one meal produced a greater and prolonged anabolic response compared to 25g — the anabolic response had no upper limit in duration in vivo in humans.

This means within your 6h window, 100g at one meal does create a longer sustained MPS window — partially compensating for fewer meals.

Practical implication: on 18:6, having a 100g protein meal at 12:00 and a 100g meal at 18:00 gives you two prolonged MPS windows covering most of the day — a workable compromise.
Updated science 2023

💡 SPECIFIC MOUNJARO + PROTEIN INTERACTION: Mounjaro slows gastric emptying — this actually HELPS protein utilization. A 65g beef meal at 12:00 stays in your stomach 4–6h on Mounjaro (vs 2–3h normal), meaning amino acid release is prolonged further. The drug inadvertently improves your per-meal protein utilization — partially compensating for fewer meal occasions in a compressed window.

02

How They Work — 3 Levels

GLP-1 agonists operate simultaneously at three levels: the stomach, the hormonal system, and the brain. Tirzepatide additionally activates GIP receptors, amplifying each effect.

Level 1 — Stomach & Gastrointestinal

01
Gastric emptying slowed
Food stays in the stomach 2–4× longer than normal. The physical sensation of fullness is dramatically prolonged. A meal that would normally pass in 2 hours takes 4–6 hours — you feel full for most of the day from a single moderate meal. This is the primary mechanism reducing caloric intake.
02
Gastric acid secretion reduced
GLP-1 reduces gastric acid production — useful side effect that reduces heartburn and GERD. On carnivore (high protein diet requiring high acid for digestion), you may need to supplement with digestive enzymes or HCL betaine to compensate for reduced acid, especially in the first 2–3 months.
03
Gut motility changes
Slowed gut transit explains constipation (most common side effect, especially on carnivore which already reduces gut transit). The entire GI tract slows — this is why nausea occurs when food can't move through fast enough. Manage with adequate hydration, magnesium, and electrolytes.

Level 2 — Hormonal System

04
Insulin secretion (glucose-dependent)
GLP-1 triggers pancreatic beta cells to release insulin — but only when blood glucose is elevated. This is the "glucose-dependent" key feature that makes it safe: unlike older diabetes drugs, it won't cause hypoglycemia when glucose is already low. On carnivore (very low glucose), this effect is minimal — the drug acts primarily through appetite and gastric mechanisms instead.
05
Glucagon suppression
Glucagon raises blood sugar by signaling the liver to release stored glucose. GLP-1 suppresses glucagon — preventing liver glucose output. On carnivore + Mounjaro, this creates a powerful dual effect: no dietary carbs entering + liver glucose output suppressed = very stable, very low blood glucose = deep, sustained ketosis. This is why the combination is particularly effective.
06
GIP receptor (tirzepatide only)
The GIP component of tirzepatide is what makes it superior to pure GLP-1 agonists. GIP improves fat cell (adipocyte) insulin sensitivity — making fat cells more responsive, enhancing fat mobilization. GIP also reduces nausea versus GLP-1 alone, which is why tirzepatide is paradoxically better tolerated despite being more effective. GIP also enhances post-meal satiety independently of GLP-1.
07
Leptin & ghrelin modulation
Ghrelin is the hunger hormone — GLP-1 agonists suppress ghrelin release, reducing baseline hunger signals. Leptin sensitivity improves as body fat decreases (leptin resistance is a primary driver of obesity). This creates a positive feedback loop: less fat → better leptin signaling → less hunger → less eating → less fat. Carnivore amplifies this by eliminating hyperpalatable foods that chronically elevate ghrelin.
08
Adiponectin increase
Adiponectin is an anti-inflammatory hormone produced by fat cells that improves insulin sensitivity and promotes fat oxidation. As body fat decreases on tirzepatide, adiponectin rises significantly — creating a self-reinforcing metabolic improvement. Higher adiponectin = better muscle insulin sensitivity = more glucose goes to muscle (not fat) = improved body composition even without additional effort.
09
Testosterone recovery
Aromatase enzyme in adipose tissue converts testosterone to estrogen. At 109kg/35% body fat, this conversion is significant — suppressing testosterone. As fat loss accelerates on tirzepatide, aromatase activity drops, testosterone rises naturally. Clinical data shows 15–30% testosterone increase with significant weight loss on GLP-1 therapy — compounding the benefits of your testosterone optimization stack.

Level 3 — Brain & Central Nervous System

10
Hypothalamic appetite suppression
GLP-1 receptors in the hypothalamus and brainstem (area postrema) directly suppress appetite signals — independently of stomach fullness. This is the "central" mechanism: even if your stomach is empty, GLP-1 reduces the drive to eat. This is why the drug works even when patients don't consciously restrict eating — appetite simply diminishes at the neurological level.
11
Reward system modulation
GLP-1 receptors in the dopamine reward pathways of the brain reduce food reward signals — making highly palatable foods less appealing. Patients commonly report that cravings for sweet, fatty, or processed foods simply disappear. On carnivore, this effect is amplified because you've already eliminated hyperpalatable foods — the combination creates a state where the desire to overeat is neurologically suppressed from two directions simultaneously.
12
Nausea center activation
The area postrema (brain's vomiting/nausea center) has dense GLP-1 receptors. High doses activate this center, causing the most common side effect: nausea. This is why slow dose escalation is critical — the brain adapts to GLP-1 signaling over weeks. Tirzepatide's GIP component partially counteracts this nausea signal, which is why tirzepatide has lower nausea rates than equivalent-efficacy doses of semaglutide.
04

05

Maximum Fat Loss + Muscle Gain

Body recomposition — losing fat and gaining muscle simultaneously — is normally very difficult. GLP-1 + carnivore + resistance training creates a rare state where both happen at the same time.

🍖
PROTEIN — the non-negotiable
200–218g protein daily at your starting weight. This is the single most important variable for preserving muscle during aggressive fat loss.

Without adequate protein, up to 40% of weight lost on GLP-1 agonists can be lean mass. With 200g+ protein + resistance training, this flips to >90% fat loss.

Distribute across 3 meals: 65g + 70g + 75g. Every gram counts.
Critical
🏋️
TRAINING — anabolic signal
5× weekly resistance training sends the mTOR signal that tells your body to preserve and build muscle even in a caloric deficit.

Progressive overload is non-negotiable: increase weight or reps every week from month 2 onwards. The muscle you build now will raise your resting metabolic rate permanently.

Prioritize compound movements: leg press, lat pulldown, chest press, hip thrust, cable row. These recruit maximum muscle fiber.
Critical
💉
MOUNJARO — metabolic amplifier
Tirzepatide improves insulin sensitivity in muscle cells — meaning amino acids and glucose are preferentially directed to muscle rather than fat storage.

GIP component specifically improves adipocyte insulin sensitivity — fat cells become more responsive, releasing fatty acids more readily for fuel.

Net effect: your body becomes dramatically better at using fat as fuel while preserving protein for muscle — the exact metabolic state needed for body recomposition.
Amplifier
🌡️
THERMAL — hormonal boost
Sauna 4–5×/week post-training: triggers up to +2,900% growth hormone release — the most powerful natural anabolic signal available without drugs.

GH preserves lean mass during caloric deficit and directly stimulates fat oxidation. This is why professional athletes use sauna as a core recovery and performance tool.

Cold 3–4×/week morning: norepinephrine +300%, BAT activation, insulin sensitivity improvement. Do NOT within 4–6h of training (blunts mTOR).
Optimizer
😴
SLEEP — where growth happens
8h minimum. During deep sleep (SWS), growth hormone is pulsatilely released — this is when muscle protein synthesis peaks and fat oxidation is highest.

Magnesium glycinate 400mg + Glycine 3–5g + Ashwagandha 600mg 30 min before bed. This stack improves SWS quality measurably within 5–7 days.

Room temperature 17–19°C — critical for sleep architecture. Warm room = poor deep sleep = reduced GH = muscle loss.
Recovery
💊
SUPPLEMENTS — the stack
Creatine 5g/day: most evidence-backed muscle preservation supplement. Also raises DHT by ~50%.

Zinc 30mg + D3 5,000IU + Ashwagandha KSM-66 600mg: natural testosterone optimization — especially important as fat loss exposes your true hormonal potential.

Caffeine 200mg pre-workout: +15–30% fat oxidation during training. Times perfectly with your protocol.

L-Carnitine 2g: transports fatty acids into mitochondria — direct fat burning enhancer.

07

Critical Caveats

💪
MUSCLE LOSS RISK
Without adequate protein + resistance training, up to 40% of weight lost on GLP-1 agonists is lean mass — not fat. This is the most underestimated risk of GLP-1 therapy.

Solution: 200g protein daily + 5× training + creatine + adequate sleep. DEXA scan every 6 months to verify fat vs lean mass ratio. Never skip protein targets even on low-appetite days.
High risk
🦋
THYROID SUPPRESSION
Aggressive caloric deficit + rapid fat loss + Mounjaro can suppress thyroid hormone production (T3 in particular). Low T3 = reduced metabolic rate = plateau + fatigue + cold intolerance.

Monitor TSH + free T3 every 3 months. If T3 drops below range, discuss with physician. Carnivore + selenium supplementation (200mcg/day) supports thyroid function.
Monitor
🩸
ELECTROLYTE DEPLETION
Your stack (Mounjaro + carnivore + 5× training + sauna + cold) creates the most aggressive electrolyte loss scenario possible. Symptoms: muscle cramps, heart palpitations, dizziness, brain fog, insomnia.

Daily non-negotiables: 3–5g sodium, 1,000–3,500mg potassium, 400mg magnesium glycinate. Double on sauna + cold days. Never skip.
Daily risk
🤢
HYPOGLYCEMIA
Mounjaro + carnivore (no carbs) + intense training + sauna all lower blood glucose. The combination can occasionally produce symptomatic hypoglycemia — dizziness, weakness, cold sweat during or after training.

Monitor glucose daily for first 3 months. If glucose <60 mg/dL with symptoms: stop training, eat a small amount of protein, rest. Report to physician.
Monitor months 1–3
🥩
DIGESTION SLOWDOWN
Mounjaro slows gastric emptying. Carnivore is already low-fiber. Combination = significant constipation risk. This causes bloating, discomfort, and can make you think you're not hungry when you're actually constipated.

Solution: 3–4L water daily, magnesium glycinate (helps motility), HCL betaine with large meat meals (helps acid production), consider digestive enzymes.
Manage proactively
⚖️
WEIGHT REGAIN RISK
Studies show 60–80% of weight regained within 1 year of stopping GLP-1 therapy without maintaining lifestyle interventions. The medication suppresses appetite neurologically — stopping it allows appetite to return rapidly.

Long-term strategy: maintain lowest effective Mounjaro dose at goal weight, continue carnivore maintenance protocol, sustain resistance training. This is not a temporary drug — it's likely a long-term commitment.
Long-term planning
⚠️ ABSOLUTE CONTRAINDICATIONS FOR MOUNJARO: Personal or family history of medullary thyroid carcinoma (MTC), Multiple Endocrine Neoplasia syndrome type 2 (MEN 2), history of pancreatitis, pregnancy or planned pregnancy. Always disclose full medical history to prescribing physician.
08

Mounjaro Exit Strategy

The most important long-term question — and the one most patients never plan for. Your specific lifestyle stack makes exit far more viable than average. Here is the complete de-escalation roadmap.

⚠️ THE BRUTAL TRUTH: Studies show a pooled mean weight regain of 9.69 kg after stopping tirzepatide/semaglutide in average patients. The drug suppresses appetite neurologically — stopping it allows appetite to reassert aggressively. The average person stopping Mounjaro has no lifestyle infrastructure. YOU have carnivore + 5× training + fasting + sauna + cold therapy + testosterone optimization — this changes the equation dramatically. But you still need a strategic exit plan.

🧠
The mindset shift
Don't think of exit as the goal. Think of it as a gradual handover from pharmacological appetite control to neurological + lifestyle appetite control.

The 18 months of transformation rewired your relationship with food, rebuilt your hormonal baseline, and permanently elevated your metabolic rate through muscle. These changes don't reverse when the drug stops — they are structural.

The muscle you built is permanent. The metabolic rate boost is permanent. The carnivore habit patterns are permanent. The fasting adaptations persist for months after stopping fasting itself.
Strategic perspective
💰
The cost argument for staying on low dose
At 2.5mg every 2 weeks (1 pen every ~4 months), annual cost at pharmacy price is ~€120–150/year.

For many people this is the optimal long-term strategy — minimal dose, minimal side effects, minimal cost, maximum insurance against regain.

Do not feel pressure to completely stop if low-dose maintenance works perfectly. The goal is your target weight and physique — not drug-free status for its own sake.
Pragmatic option
🔄
If you regain — the re-initiation protocol
If weight rebounds >3kg after exit: re-initiate immediately at 2.5mg weekly. Do not wait, do not try to diet it off alone first, do not feel shame.

Obesity has a biological set point that reasserts itself — this is neurological, not a willpower failure. Re-initiation at low dose quickly reverses regain before it compounds.

Many patients do 2–3 cycles of use/exit over their lifetime — each cycle improves their lifestyle infrastructure and reduces the minimum effective maintenance dose needed.
Re-initiation plan
🎯 BOTTOM LINE: Your specific stack (carnivore + 5× training + thermal therapy + testosterone optimization + strategic fasting) gives you the highest realistic chance of successfully exiting Mounjaro of any patient profile. The 18-month transformation permanently rebuilds your metabolism. Exit gradually over 12–18 months, activate your natural GLP-1 replacement stack progressively, and keep 2.5mg biweekly as a safety net. This is not a race to zero — it is a strategic handover from drug to lifestyle.
12

Sleep & Testosterone

Sleep quality and testosterone are deeply interconnected — and both are directly impacted by your stack (Mounjaro + carnivore + 5× training + thermal therapy). Optimizing both simultaneously gives your transformation its highest ceiling.

🔑 THE MASTER INSIGHT: The single biggest sleep AND testosterone lever is not any supplement — it is fat loss itself. Aromatase enzyme in adipose tissue converts testosterone to estrogen and disrupts sleep architecture. Every kilogram lost on Mounjaro naturally raises testosterone and improves sleep quality. Supplements accelerate and optimize — but your transformation IS the primary intervention.

Dual-purpose supplements — boost BOTH sleep AND testosterone simultaneously

SupplementDoseSleep benefitTestosterone benefitPriority
Magnesium glycinate400mg before bedDeep sleep, muscle relaxation, lowers body temp — glycine component is itself a sleep promoterReduces SHBG (which binds/inactivates T), raises free testosterone — especially in athletesNon-negotiable
Ashwagandha KSM-66600mg before bedCortisol reduction — high cortisol from 5× training + Mounjaro metabolic stress is primary sleep disruptorClinical trials show 10–22% testosterone increase over 8–12 weeks by lowering cortisol (T's enemy)Non-negotiable
Vitamin D3 + K25,000 IU + 100mcg morningRegulates circadian rhythm — paradoxically critical for night sleep. D3 deficiency = disrupted sleep architectureD3 acts as steroid hormone precursor — directly stimulates Leydig cells in testes. Deficiency at 109kg almost certain.Non-negotiable
Zinc bisglycinate30–40mg before bedSupports sleep hormone regulation — zinc is cofactor in melatonin synthesisMost important testosterone mineral. Carnivore + sweating + Mounjaro depletes zinc rapidly. Deficiency directly suppresses T.Non-negotiable

Sleep-specific supplements

🧂
Glycine (standalone)
Dose: 3–5g in water 30 min before bed

Lowers core body temperature (essential for deep sleep onset), reduces sleep latency, improves REM quality.

Fully carnivore-compliant — amino acid found naturally in collagen and bone broth. One of the most underrated sleep supplements with strong clinical evidence. Works within 3–5 days.
Add immediately
🍵
L-Theanine
Dose: 200mg before bed

Promotes alpha brain waves — relaxed but alert state, reduces sleep-onset anxiety without sedation. Pairs excellently with magnesium glycinate for a synergistic calming effect.

Carnivore-compatible. Works within 1–2 days. Particularly useful on high-stress days or when post-workout cortisol is elevated.
High value
🌙
Melatonin — use sparingly
Dose: 0.5mg maximum (not 5–10mg as commonly sold)

Most people severely overdose melatonin. The effective physiological dose is 0.2–0.5mg — higher doses cause next-day grogginess and suppress natural melatonin production over time.

Use only for: jet lag, shift work, circadian rhythm reset. Never use nightly long-term.
Situational only

Physical environment — often more impactful than supplements

ToolWhy it worksTarget
Room temperatureCore body temperature must drop 1–2°C for deep sleep onset — warm room prevents this entirely17–19°C — non-negotiable
Blackout mask (3D contoured)Any light exposure suppresses melatonin — especially critical with Mounjaro + carnivore creating a strong cortisol/melatonin cycleComplete darkness
Weighted blanket (6–9kg)Deep pressure stimulation reduces cortisol, increases serotonin and oxytocin — particularly effective for high-training-volume individuals with elevated cortisol6–9kg body weight appropriate
Magnesium chloride bath500g flakes in warm bath 1–2h before bed on heavy training days — transdermal magnesium + thermal cooling effect = excellent sleep onsetHeavy training days
Sleep durationGH released during deep sleep — this is when muscle protein synthesis peaks and fat oxidation is highest. Insufficient sleep = muscle loss + fat gain regardless of diet.8h minimum — absolute priority

Testosterone-specific supplements — beyond the dual-purpose stack

⚡
Boron glycinate
Dose: 6–10mg with food

The most underrated testosterone supplement. Reduces SHBG (Sex Hormone Binding Globulin) — SHBG binds testosterone making it biologically inactive. Less SHBG = more FREE testosterone available to tissues.

Carnivore provides almost zero boron (found mainly in plant foods). Studies show significant SHBG reduction and free T increase within 1–2 weeks. Combines synergistically with zinc.
Highly underrated
🌿
Tongkat Ali (Longjack)
Dose: 400mg standardized extract (2% eurycomanone) — morning

Dual action: raises LH (signals testes to produce more testosterone) AND reduces SHBG. Studies show 15–37% testosterone increase over 12 weeks in men with low-normal testosterone.

Synergizes extremely well with Fadogia Agrestis — the two together cover both the production and bioavailability sides of testosterone optimization.
Strong evidence
🔬
Fadogia Agrestis
Dose: 600mg with food. Cycle: 8 weeks on / 4 weeks off

Stimulates LH (luteinizing hormone) — the signal that tells your testes to produce testosterone. Strong anecdotal + animal evidence, growing human data.

Use cautiously — long-term human safety data still limited. Cycle to prevent receptor desensitization. Pairs with Tongkat Ali for maximum LH stimulation.
Cycle — 8/4 weeks
💊
Creatine 5g/day
Already in your stack — but the testosterone angle is often missed.

Creatine raises DHT (dihydrotestosterone) by approximately 50% — DHT is 3–5× more potent than testosterone at the androgen receptor. More DHT = more androgenic effect on muscle growth, strength, and body composition.

Take any time — with or without food, pre or post workout.
Already in stack
