Injection Timing — Day & Hour

Morning vs night, which day of the week, injection site rotation — every practical detail for your 5× weekly training protocol.

📋 OFFICIAL FDA ANSWER: Administer once weekly at any time of day, with or without meals. Tirzepatide has a half-life of ~5 days — stable therapeutic levels are maintained in blood throughout the week regardless of injection time. BUT for your specific 5× training protocol, timing DOES matter practically for side effect management.

Morning vs evening — comparison for your protocol

TimingPros for your stackCons for your stackVerdict
Evening / Night Sleep through initial nausea — GI side effects typically appear 1–2 days post-injection. Evening injection lets you sleep through early discomfort Can't monitor side effects in real time ✅ Better for your protocol
Morning Monitor side effects during the day, full alertness for injection, routine-building with morning habits Nausea may hit during a training session 24–48h later if injecting on a training day morning ⚠️ Fine if injecting day before rest day

Day of the week — impact on your 5× training schedule

Side effects peak 24–48 hours after injection. With Mon/Tue/Thu/Fri/Sat training, you need injection day to protect all training sessions.

Inject daySide effects peakTraining days affectedVerdict
Friday eveningSaturday–Sunday✅ None — weekend rest + contrast therapy days⭐ OPTIMAL for your protocol
Thursday eveningFriday–Saturday⚠️ Friday training may be affectedGood backup option
Saturday eveningSunday–Monday⚠️ Monday training potentially affectedAcceptable
Sunday eveningMonday–Tuesday❌ Two key training days impactedAvoid
Wednesday eveningThursday–Friday❌ Thursday training affectedAvoid

Your optimal injection protocol — all factors combined

📅
WHEN — Friday Evening 18:00–20:00
After dinner, end of eating window (18:6 protocol)

Why Friday evening is perfect for your stack:

✅ Training protection — side effects peak Saturday + Sunday (your rest + contrast therapy days). All 5 training days completely unaffected.

✅ Fasting synergy — if you do a 24h fast Friday (dinner→dinner), inject immediately after breaking fast. Drug enters system when insulin is already suppressed and fat oxidation is elevated — enhancing first 48h post-injection fat burning effect.

✅ Contrast therapy Saturday — sauna + cold therapy on Saturday actually reduces GI side effects by improving circulation and systemic inflammation.

✅ Weekly anchor — every Friday evening is Mounjaro day. Clear, memorable, impossible to forget.

✅ Steady state alignment — by Monday morning the drug is 48h in and appetite suppression is fully active for the training week ahead.
Recommended — your protocol
📍
WHERE — Injection Site Rotation
Rotate every week to prevent lipohypertrophy (fatty lumps from repeated injections at same site).

Week 1: Abdomen left (5cm from navel)
Week 2: Thigh right (mid outer thigh)
Week 3: Abdomen right
Week 4: Thigh left
Week 5: Upper arm left (need assistance)
Week 6: Upper arm right
Then repeat cycle.

Abdomen is most practical for self-injection — inject at least 5cm away from navel, avoid scars, bruises or skin folds.
Rotate every week
⚙️
HOW — Injection technique tips
Temperature: Remove from fridge 30 min before injection — room temperature is less painful than cold pen.

Skin prep: Clean injection site with alcohol wipe, let dry completely before injecting.

Technique: Pinch skin lightly, insert pen flat against skin, press and hold button for full 10 seconds until click confirmed.

Post-injection: Do not rub the injection site — can cause bruising and uneven absorption.

Storage: Fridge 2–8°C in original box (protects from light). Never freeze. Can store at room temperature <30°C for up to 30 days once removed from fridge.
Technique matters

Critical rules — missed doses, changing days, special situations

SituationWhat to do
Missed dose — less than 4 days lateTake immediately as soon as you remember
Missed dose — more than 4 days lateSkip entirely — wait for next scheduled dose. Never double dose.
Changing injection day permanentlyMinimum 72 hours (3 days) between old and new dose
Before surgery or procedureCRITICAL — tell your surgeon/anaesthetist you are on Mounjaro. Gastric emptying is slowed — increases risk of food aspiration under anaesthesia. May need to stop injection 1–2 weeks before procedure.
Travel / time zone changeKeep to same 24h window — time zone shifts of <12h are fine. For major time differences, shift injection time gradually over 2–3 weeks.
Pen dropped on hard surfaceDo not use — glass components may have cracked internally. Use new pen.
Frozen pen (accidentally)Discard immediately — freezing denatures the protein structure. Never use a frozen pen.
Pen left at room temp too longSafe up to 30 days at <30°C. If in doubt about time or temperature — discard and use new pen.

⚠️ SURGERY WARNING: Mounjaro significantly slows gastric emptying. Before ANY surgery, procedure, or even a dental procedure requiring sedation — inform your healthcare provider you are on Mounjaro. Reaching 10–12% Body Fat

10–12% body fat is fitness model / competitive physique territory — achievable but requires a fundamentally different approach than fat loss. This is the complete roadmap from your current protocol to elite body composition.

🏆
What 10–12% looks like
Full six-pack visible at rest — not just when flexed
Vascularity in arms, shoulders, forearms permanently visible
Muscle separation clearly defined across chest, back, shoulders
Face sharp and angular — jaw and cheekbones prominent
Forearm veins visible at all times

This represents the top 2–3% of male body composition globally. Most gym-going men never reach it. It requires precision, patience, and a Phase 2 gear shift beyond your current protocol.
Elite territory
⚠️
The critical reality shift
35% → 16% is primarily a fat loss problem — solved by Mounjaro + carnivore + training. Aggressive deficit works.

16% → 10% is primarily a body recomposition + precision problem. Aggressive deficit stops working and starts destroying muscle.

Metabolic adaptation kicks in: BMR can suppress by 10–20% during prolonged cuts. Hormones (T, leptin, GH) all drop. Hunger becomes intense. Your body fights back harder the leaner you get.

Different phase = different tools = different mindset.
Phase shift required
📊
10% vs 12% — the real difference
Going from 12% → 10% requires as much effort as going from 16% → 12%. The last 2% is exponentially harder.

Most men are genuinely happy at 12% and find 10% requires lifestyle sacrifice not worth the marginal visual difference.

Honest recommendation: target 12% as your first milestone. Evaluate at that point whether the additional precision and sacrifice of reaching 10% aligns with your life. Both are elite achievements.
Know your ceiling

The 3-phase roadmap — from 35% to 10%

Phase 1
35%→16%
Fat loss phase — your current protocol
Duration Months 1–20

Hormonal optimization — becomes the bottleneck at sub-15%

💪
Testosterone — the critical lever
At aggressive deficits below 15% body fat, testosterone drops significantly — making it nearly impossible to build the muscle needed to drop below 13%.

Natural stack maximized: Zinc + D3 + Ashwagandha + Boron + Tongkat Ali + Fadogia

If total T remains below 400 ng/dL after 6 months of Phase 2: Testavan or Testosterone Enanthate becomes highly justified and valuable here — not just for libido but as a genuine body recomposition tool.

High testosterone = better nitrogen retention = more muscle preserved during cut = lower body fat % achievable.
Critical at sub-15%
🦋
Leptin — the metabolic regulator
Leptin is produced by fat cells — as fat decreases below 15%, leptin crashes → hunger becomes intense → metabolic rate suppresses → fat loss stalls.

Solutions:
Diet breaks every 8 weeks (2 weeks at maintenance) — temporarily restore leptin
Strategic carb days — brief insulin/leptin restoration
Quality sleep 8h — leptin is restored during sleep

Without leptin management, most people plateau permanently at 14–15% and cannot break through.
Manage proactively
😴
GH + Sleep — non-negotiable
Studies show people in caloric deficits sleeping 5.5h lose 55% less fat and 60% more muscle than those sleeping 8.5h.

At sub-15% body fat this effect is even more pronounced — GH released during deep sleep is your primary muscle preservation mechanism when dietary intake is restricted.

Your pre-bed sauna → magnesium glycinate + glycine + ashwagandha → 17–19°C room → 8h sleep protocol becomes absolutely non-negotiable in Phase 2. It is not optional recovery — it is active body recomposition.
Non-negotiable
📈
Cortisol — the enemy at low body fat
Every additional % fat loss at advanced leanness requires more dietary stress → more cortisol → more muscle breakdown → paradoxically harder to get leaner.

Cortisol management tools:
Ashwagandha KSM-66 600mg (most evidence-backed)
Phosphatidylserine 400mg/day (blunts cortisol post-training)
Meditation / stress management
Training volume not exceeding recovery capacity
Cold exposure (acute cortisol spike → chronic cortisol reduction)
Manage aggressively

HIIT protocol to add in Phase 2 (post-resistance training)

⚡
Standard HIIT — 2–3×/week
Protocol: 6–8 rounds × 20 sec max effort / 40 sec rest
Total time: 15–20 min
Equipment: Sprint, bike, rowing machine, battle ropes
When: Immediately after resistance training — not a separate session

HIIT is superior to LISS cardio for fat reduction per unit time due to higher EPOC (afterburn) and greater metabolic adaptation. 20 min HIIT = equivalent calorie burn as 40–45 min moderate steady state.
Add from Phase 2
🚴
LISS cardio — 1–2×/week
Protocol: 30–40 min at 60–65% max heart rate
Equipment: Bike, walking incline treadmill, elliptical
When: Rest days or morning fasted

LISS is preferable for recovery-sensitive individuals and older adults. Combines well with cold bath on rest day mornings — cold bath fasted then 30 min LISS = maximum fat oxidation window with dual BAT and fat mobilization activation.
Rest day option
📐
Periodization — prevent plateaus
Wave loading for resistance training:
Week 1–3: Heavy (5–6 reps, 85% 1RM)
Week 4: Deload (50% volume, 60% weight)
Week 5–7: Moderate (8–12 reps, 70–75% 1RM)
Week 8: Diet break (reduce volume)

This prevents CNS burnout during the long Phase 2 cut while maintaining progressive overload for muscle preservation and growth.
Prevents plateaus
🎯 FINAL HONEST ASSESSMENT: Your protocol — Mounjaro + carnivore + 5× training + thermal therapy + fasting — is fully capable of taking you to 10–12% body fat. The journey takes approximately 36–42 months of consistent execution. At 12% you will have a physique most men never achieve in their lifetime. Evaluate at 12% whether pursuing 10% is worth the additional precision and sacrifice. Both are extraordinary achievements from a 109kg starting point.

TRT — Testosterone Replacement Therapy

The most powerful legal body recomposition tool available. Combined with your Mounjaro + carnivore + training stack, TRT acts as a hormonal force multiplier — amplifying every fat loss and muscle building mechanism simultaneously through 9 independent pathways.

🔬 2025–2026 RESEARCH CONFIRMS: A narrative review specifically evaluated the intersection between GLP-1 receptor agonist therapy (tirzepatide) and TRT — concluding that TRT added to GLP-1 therapy further ameliorates weight-loss-induced reduction of muscle mass in men with obesity and hypogonadism. This is your exact clinical situation. TRT + Mounjaro is specifically validated by the latest science.

Clinical evidence — what TRT actually produces at therapeutic doses

🔥
Fat loss — direct and significant
Clinical trials show testosterone treatment in obese men produces 2.9 kg greater fat mass loss compared to placebo over 56 weeks at standard TRT doses with no dietary optimization.

With your carnivore + Mounjaro + thermal stack, this number multiplies significantly. TRT reduces visceral fat (the kind surrounding organs) most powerfully — visceral fat has dense androgen receptors that T directly signals to shrink.

Subcutaneous fat also decreases but more slowly. Improved insulin sensitivity, enhanced metabolism, and higher basal metabolic rate mean the body burns more calories at rest permanently.
+2.9 kg fat loss vs placebo
💪
Muscle gain — remarkable numbers
In just 10 weeks, men taking testosterone can gain roughly 3 kg of fat-free mass without even exercising. With resistance training added, results can double to ~6 kg in 10 weeks — achieving in just over a month what natural men expect with years of gym work.

At TRT doses (150–200mg/week — not bodybuilder doses), results are more modest but still significant — especially combined with your 5× weekly resistance training.

One of the most notable effects is simultaneous fat reduction + lean mass increase — body recomposition that is essentially impossible to achieve naturally at this speed.
+3–6 kg lean mass / 10 weeks
⚡
The lifestyle amplification effect
TRT is not a magic shortcut — it is a hormonal foundation that amplifies everything your lifestyle already does.

Every training session produces more MPS. Every cold bath produces more norepinephrine response. Every sauna session produces more GH utilization. Every protein meal generates more muscle protein synthesis. Every Mounjaro dose improves insulin sensitivity that TRT compounds further.

Your protocol already has the best lifestyle stack possible — TRT removes the hormonal ceiling that was limiting your results.
Force multiplier

What to stock — Androtardyl injectable
Product: Androtardyl 250mg/1ml (Testosterone Enanthate) — French standard
Shelf life: 3–5 years at room temperature away from light
Storage: Room temp <25°C, dark, original packaging. No refrigeration needed.

Quantity for 12 months at 150mg/week:
150mg/week = 0.6ml per injection × 52 weeks = ~31ml = ~31 vials (1ml each)
Buy 40–50 vials for safety margin

Critical caveats — what TRT does NOT do and risks to manage

⚠️
Permanent shutdown of natural production
TRT permanently suppresses LH and FSH → your testes stop producing testosterone. This is expected and normal but means:

Never stop cold turkey — causes severe hypogonadism worse than before starting. If you want to stop, a proper PCT (HCG + Clomid/Enclomiphene) over 3–4 months is required.

Plan for long-term use — most men who start TRT stay on it indefinitely. This is not a problem — it's a commitment.
Never stop abruptly
🩺
Estrogen management — the most common mistake
Many doctors only test total testosterone and ignore estradiol. Insist on estradiol (E2) testing every 6 weeks initially.

High E2 from aromatization causes: water retention (looks fat not muscular), mood instability, fat gain — directly counteracting TRT benefits.

Only add aromatase inhibitor if E2 above 40 pg/mL — never prophylactically. Crashed estrogen (E2 below 15) is worse than high estrogen: joint pain, mood collapse, libido destruction.
Monitor E2 every 6 weeks
🩸
Hematocrit — the cardiovascular risk
TRT stimulates red blood cell production (erythropoiesis) — beneficial for training performance but dangerous above 52% hematocrit (blood becomes too thick → clot risk).

Check hematocrit every 3 months — non-negotiable.

If above 52%: donate blood (reduces RBC count immediately), reduce dose temporarily, or increase hydration. Never ignore elevated hematocrit — it is the primary cardiovascular risk of TRT.
Check every 3 months
🎯
TRT is not a shortcut — it's a multiplier
At 150–200mg/week you are replacing physiological testosterone — not going supraphysiological like bodybuilders at 500–1000mg/week. Results are meaningful and significant — but not miraculous overnight.

Timeline reality: Mood and energy improve in weeks 1–4. Body composition changes become measurable at month 3. Full physique transformation takes 6–12 months.

Your lifestyle protocol is the engine — TRT is the turbocharger.
Multiplier not magic

Maintain T without shutdownEnclomiphene (SERM)Raises T by blocking estrogen receptors in hypothalamus → natural LH/FSH preserved. Growing option for men wanting T optimization without fertility impact.
Restore fertility after TRTPCT: HCG + Clomid/Enclomiphene 3–4 monthsRestart natural production. Takes 3–6 months. Not guaranteed to fully restore — consider before starting TRT if fertility is a near-term goal.

Complete Blood Work Guide

Every parameter to test, optimal target values, why each matters for your protocol, how to get them in France (médecin traitant vs endocrinologist vs direct lab), estimated costs, and exact timing. Everything in one place — bring this to your appointment.

⏰ GOLDEN RULE: All blood tests must be done fasting minimum 12 hours and before 10:00 AM. Testosterone follows a diurnal rhythm peaking in the morning — any test after 10am significantly underestimates your true level. Glucose, insulin, and lipids also require fasted state for accuracy. Same time, same conditions, every test for valid comparisons.

Getting the blood test in France — 3 routes compared

👨‍⚕️
Route 1 — Médecin traitant
Cost: €30–80 patient cost after Sécu remboursement
Coverage: 60–70% by Sécu for most markers
Speed: Need appointment first (1–3 weeks typically)

What to say word-for-word:
"Je souhaite un bilan complet incluant : NFS + hématocrite, glycémie à jeun, insuline à jeun, HbA1c, bilan lipidique complet avec ApoB, ALAT, ASAT, créatinine + DFG estimé, TSH + T3L + T4L, testostérone totale et libre, LH, FSH, SHBG, estradiol, prolactine, PSA."

Risk: Some GPs may refuse to prescribe all hormonal markers simultaneously — may refer to endocrinologist for hormonal panel.
Cheapest — may need persistence
🏥
Route 2 — Endocrinologue ⭐ Best
Cost: €50–100 consultation after Sécu (Secteur 1 = lowest cost)
Coverage: 60–70% by Sécu
Speed: Doctolib appointment — 2–6 weeks typically

Why it's the best route:
Endocrinologists prescribe the FULL hormonal panel without question — this is their specialty. They will prescribe total T, free T, LH, FSH, SHBG, E2, prolactin all in one ordonnance. They will also issue the TRT prescription when blood work confirms hypogonadism.

One appointment covers: full diagnosis + full blood panel + TRT prescription if indicated

👉 doctolib.fr/endocrinologue → filter "Secteur 1" → nearest to Annemasse
Best overall — one stop
🔬
Route 3 — Direct lab (sans ordonnance)
Cost: €150–280 out of pocket — no Sécu coverage
Coverage: 0% — fully self-pay
Speed: ✅ Immediate — book online, go same week

Best labs near Annemasse / Geneva border:
• Synlab (synlab.fr) — largest network, full hormonal panels available

Best for: Getting baseline results immediately without waiting for GP/endocrinologist appointment
Fastest — no doctor needed

Important note — BIA scale accuracy

Understanding your scale readings
Your Renpho scale shows two different fat readings — 27.4% body fat (calculated) and 35.3 fat level score (proprietary composite). This discrepancy is normal and expected.

Bioelectrical impedance (BIA) scales consistently underestimate body fat at higher weights — the electrical signal cannot accurately penetrate large fat deposits. The true body fat % is likely 30–32% — between the two readings.

Solution: Use the scale primarily for trend tracking not absolute numbers. The direction and rate of change over time is what matters — not the absolute value on any given day. A DEXA scan every 6 months will give you the true fat/lean mass breakdown.

Most reliable metrics from your scale: Weight (very accurate), muscle mass trend, visceral fat score trend, BMR trend. Use circumferences as your primary body composition indicator between DEXA scans.

Collagen deep dive — especially important for your transformation

🦴
Why collagen matters at 108kg
At 108kg with aggressive weight loss ahead, collagen has 3 critical roles:

1. Joint protection: 5× weekly training puts enormous load on tendons and ligaments. Collagen is the structural protein of connective tissue — supplementation directly supports repair.

2. Skin elasticity: Losing 35+ kg rapidly creates a risk of loose skin. Collagen peptides support skin structural integrity during rapid fat loss — essential to minimize this effect.

3. Gut health: Carnivore + Mounjaro changes gut environment significantly. Collagen (specifically glycine) supports intestinal lining integrity.
3 critical roles for you
⏱️
Optimal collagen timing — pre-training is best
Research shows collagen synthesis in tendons peaks when collagen peptides are consumed 30–60 min before exercise combined with vitamin C (50mg).

Pre-training (best for joints): 10–15g collagen + 50mg Vit C → 30 min later → train

Pre-bed (best for skin + sleep): Collagen provides glycine → compounds your bedtime glycine powder for deeper sleep and overnight skin repair

Both? Yes: 10g pre-training + 10g pre-bed = 20g/day for maximum joint + skin + sleep benefit
Pre-training timing = science-backed
🥩
Best collagen types for your protocol
Type I + III (bovine hydrolyzed): Skin, tendons, ligaments, bones — the most relevant for your goals. Most widely available on Amazon.fr.

Type II (chicken/marine): Specifically for cartilage — useful if knee or hip discomfort develops under heavy training load.

What to look for: "Hydrolyzed" or "peptides" on label — this means pre-digested into smaller peptides for superior absorption vs plain gelatin.

Carnivore compatible: ✅ Yes — fully animal-derived. Bovine collagen from grass-fed sources is the gold standard.
Bovine hydrolyzed = best choice

⛔ What NOT to take — disqualified supplements for your protocol

SupplementWhy avoid
Whey protein powderInsulin index 109 (higher than white bread) — destroys ketosis, disrupts GKI, blocks fat loss. Replace with beef protein isolate (Beef-XP or Beef Zero).
Tribulus TerrestrisZero clinical evidence for testosterone increase in humans. 2025 meta-analysis confirms ineffective. Marketing hype only. Weak libido effect only — better alternatives exist.
Ketone supplements (Ketone IQ) for fat lossExogenous ketones temporarily SUPPRESS your own fat burning — body uses dietary ketones instead of mobilizing stored fat. Only use situationally for energy/mental performance, never as a fat loss tool.
Any plant-based protein powderOff carnivore protocol. Soy specifically contains phytoestrogens that directly suppress testosterone — the opposite of what you need.
Berberine pre/post-workoutNot a supplement to avoid — but a timing error that blunts mTOR muscle building signal. Always with meals, never within 2h of training.
High-dose melatonin (5–10mg)10–20× above physiological dose. Causes next-day grogginess, suppresses natural production over time. 0.5mg is the correct dose.
Calcium supplementsYour D3 + K2 directs dietary calcium to bones. Additional calcium supplementation competes with zinc absorption and increases cardiovascular calcification risk without K2 guidance.
💊 SUPPLEMENT PHILOSOPHY: Supplements fill gaps that your carnivore diet creates (boron, D3, electrolytes), amplify what your protocol already does (creatine + training, collagen + training, ashwagandha + sleep), and bridge timing windows where food alone cannot deliver the therapeutic dose (berberine timing, pre-training collagen). They are not shortcuts — they are precision tools that multiply the effectiveness of an already excellent protocol. Start with Tier 1 this week. Add Tier 2 in Month 1. Add Tier 3 in Month 2. Your total investment of ~€235/month optimizes a transformation protocol worth infinitely more. 🎯

Hematocrit — Complete Guide

What hematocrit is, why yours is elevated at 50.3%, why it matters critically for TRT, and exactly how to lower it — complete action plan.

What is hematocrit — the simple explanation

🩸
The river analogy
Think of your blood as a river. Hematocrit tells you how thick that river is — specifically the percentage of your total blood volume occupied by red blood cells.

Normal blood: ~45% red blood cells, ~55% plasma (fluid)
Your blood (50.3%): More red blood cells, less fluid — thicker blood

Thicker blood flows less efficiently through small capillaries and dramatically increases clot risk when hematocrit exceeds 52%.
Simple concept
⚠️
Why TRT raises hematocrit
Testosterone stimulates erythropoiesis — red blood cell production in bone marrow. This is the same mechanism elite athletes exploit with EPO doping.

More RBC = higher hematocrit = thicker blood. At physiological TRT doses (150mg/week), hematocrit typically rises 3–5 points within 3–6 months.

Your problem: You're already at 50.3% — adding 3–5 points puts you at 53–55% = genuinely dangerous clot risk territory.
TRT specific risk
🎯
The danger of high hematocrit
When blood becomes too viscous it flows poorly through capillaries and micro-vessels, massively increasing risk of:

• Deep vein thrombosis (DVT)
• Pulmonary embolism
• Stroke
• Heart attack

This is why the 52% threshold is non-negotiable — above it, blood donation is mandatory before the next TRT injection.
Life-threatening risk

Hematocrit ranges — where you are and where you need to be

HematocritZoneRiskAction
39–45%✅ OptimalNoneIdeal target zone on TRT
45–48.6%✅ NormalMinimalFine — no action needed
50.3% (you now)⚠️ Above normalLow but realInvestigate cause. Retest hydrated. Do not start TRT until discussed with doctor.
48.7–51.9%⚠️ ElevatedModerateHydration + sleep apnoea test + monitor every 6 weeks on TRT
52%🚨 TRT danger thresholdHighDonate blood immediately (450ml). Reduce TRT dose. Do not inject until normalized.
>54%❌ CriticalVery highStop TRT. Urgent medical review. Emergency blood donation.

Why your hematocrit is 50.3% — 3 likely causes

💧
Cause 1 — Dehydration (most likely)
Your blood was drawn at 07h40 after overnight fast. Carnivore diet + morning dehydration artificially concentrates blood cells, making hematocrit appear higher than true value.

Test: Drink 1.5–2L water in the 2 hours before next blood test. Many men see hematocrit drop 2–3 points simply by being properly hydrated.

Verdict: This is the most likely explanation. Rule this out first before any other intervention.
Rule out first
😴
Cause 2 — Sleep apnoea (high probability)
At 108kg, sleep apnoea prevalence is 40–70%. Chronic nighttime oxygen desaturation → body produces more RBC to compensate → elevated hematocrit.

Your neck circumference is 40cm — above the 37cm sleep apnoea risk threshold.

Signs: Snoring, unrefreshed sleep, daytime fatigue, partner reports breathing pauses.

Action: Ask GP for polygraphie ventilatoire nocturne (home sleep study) — covered by Sécu at your BMI. CPAP normalizes oxygen → hematocrit drops naturally within weeks.
High probability — investigate
🧬
Cause 3 — Natural high (possible)
Some men naturally run hematocrit in the 48–52% range — particularly those with higher athletic baseline, higher altitude dwelling, or genetic predisposition to erythropoiesis.

Annemasse at 430m altitude is not a significant factor. But if dehydration and sleep apnoea are ruled out and hematocrit remains elevated, this is your baseline.

Implication: If truly a natural high, Testavan gel is safer than injections for TRT (less erythropoietic stimulus) and blood donation every 8–12 weeks becomes your standard management.
Rule out after others

TRT Administration & Storage Protocol

Your complete operational manual for testosterone enanthate (Androtardyl 250mg/ml) — route of administration, dosing protocol, ampoule rotation, syringe storage, summer heat management, and the prerequisite safety sequence before initiation.

🚫 PREREQUISITE — NON-NEGOTIABLE: Do not initiate TRT until hematocrit is normalized. Your baseline of 50.3% is only 1.7 points from the 52% danger threshold. Required sequence: (1) EFS blood donation, (2) wait 4–6 weeks, (3) retest hematocrit hydrated, fasted, before 10am, (4) confirm hematocrit <48%, (5) complete hormonal panel (Section 17), (6) Geneva consultation, (7) prescription, (8) only then purchase stock. See Section 20 for full hematocrit management.

Route of administration — Subcutaneous vs Intramuscular

FactorIntramuscular (IM)Subcutaneous (SubQ)Winner for your case
Needle23G × 30mm (glute) or 25G × 25mm (deltoid)29G × 12mm insulin syringeSubQ — much less painful
Half-life of testosterone enanthate~4.5 days~8 days (slower release)SubQ — smoother profile
Peak serum T post-injection24–48 hours (sharp peak)48–72 hours (rounded peak)SubQ — lower peaks
Hematocrit rise over 6 months3–4 percentage points (weekly)1.5–2.5 points at equivalent doseSubQ — critical for you at 50.3%
Estradiol conversionHigher peak E2 at injection day 2–330–40% less E2 at equivalent TSubQ — protects body comp
Injection comfortNoticeable pain, occasional PIP 1–3 daysMinimal pain, comparable to insulinSubQ

Long-term tissue impactOccasional muscle nodules with repeated useNo nodule formationSubQ

💡 CONCLUSION FOR YOUR CASE: SubQ wins on 8 of 10 dimensions. The two factors favoring IM (volume tolerance, prescriber familiarity) don't apply at your dose. SubQ's lower hematocrit pressure is the decisive factor given your 50.3% baseline. The molecule and dose are identical regardless of route — only the injection site changes.

Your protocol — 83.3mg twice weekly SubQ (~167mg/week)

💉
The math — one ampoule = three injections
Ampoule: Androtardyl 250mg / 1ml
Per injection: 83.3mg = 0.333ml (exactly one-third of an ampoule)
Frequency: Twice weekly, 3.5 days apart (e.g., Monday morning + Thursday evening)
Weekly total: ~167mg
Ampoule duration: 10.5 days (3 injections × 3.5 days)
Annual consumption: 34.7 ampoules → order 38–40 with buffer

Annual cost: 38 × €17.50 = ~€665, with Sécu reimbursement (65%) → ~€233 out-of-pocket
Optimal protocol
🎯
Why this dose is right for you
Projected serum T: 850–1050 ng/dL (excellent recomp range)
Hematocrit pressure: Moderate (~3 point rise / 6 months) — manageable with quarterly blood donation
Estradiol management: Typically does not require an aromatase inhibitor
Body recomp efficiency: 95% of what 250mg/week delivers, with 40% less side effect burden

Going above 180mg/week gives marginal recomp benefit with disproportionate side effect cost. The 167mg/week dose sits at the sweet spot of the dose-response curve.
Sweet spot
📍
Injection site rotation
Primary site: Subcutaneous abdominal fat, 5cm lateral to navel
Rotation: Alternate left and right sides each injection
Backup sites: Upper outer thigh, flank (love handle area)

Why abdomen: Easy access seated, fully clothed (just lift shirt), ample subcutaneous fat, well-vascularized for absorption, minimal anatomical risk (no major vessels or nerves in superficial fat).

Avoid injecting within 2cm of the navel, within 5cm of any scar, or directly into a vein visible at the surface.
Rotate every injection

Ampoule opening & three-syringe pre-draw protocol

StepActionWhy it matters
1Wash hands thoroughly with soap, dry with clean towelHand microbes are the #1 contamination source
2Wipe ampoule neck with alcohol swab, allow to air dry 10 secondsAlcohol kills surface bacteria at the break point
3Wrap ampoule neck in clean tissue, snap firmly away from facePrevents cuts and protects eyes from glass
4Attach 21G filter needle (5-micron) to first syringe, draw 0.333mlCRITICAL — filter removes microscopic glass shards from the snap
5Detach filter needle, attach fresh 29G × 12mm insulin needle, capTwo-needle technique — never inject with the drawing needle
6Repeat with 2nd syringe (filter draw 0.333ml, swap to insulin needle, cap)All draws done in one sterile session
7Repeat with 3rd syringe — empties the ampouleZero wastage, perfect ampoule efficiency
8Label each syringe: date drawn + intended injection dateTracking prevents confusion and missed/double doses
9Inject Syringe 1 immediately; store Syringes 2 & 3Storage clock starts now — use within 10 days
10Dispose of glass ampoule and filter needle in DASRI sharps containerFree from pharmacy with prescription
⚠️ FILTER NEEDLE IS NON-NEGOTIABLE: Snapping a glass ampoule deposits microscopic glass shards into the oil. Injecting these into subcutaneous tissue causes granulomas, sterile abscesses, and chronic inflammation. Filter needles (BD Blunt Fill 5-micron or equivalent) cost €15–25 per box of 100. Order from pharmaceutical supply if your pharmacy doesn't stock them.

Ampoule storage — temperature management year-round

TemperatureDuration acceptableStorage strategyRisk level
15–25°C (manufacturer spec)5 years (full shelf life)Original carton, cool dark drawer, north-facing room✅ Optimal
25–30°C12+ months without meaningful degradationDefault storage, no special action needed✅ Safe

Annemasse summer protocol — three-tier heat management

🏠
Tier 1 — Default location
Best spots in your home:
• North-facing bedroom drawer (interior wall)
• Ground-floor hallway closet
• Under-bed storage in cool room
• Basement or cave if you have one

Avoid: south/west-facing rooms, top floor, attics, near windows, near appliances (TVs, computers, lamps, kitchen heat sources).

This alone keeps the storage location 4–6°C cooler than the rest of the house.
10 months/year sufficient
🧊
Tier 2 — Insulated container (summer)
What to buy: Small insulated cooler bag (€10–20 at Décathlon or Carrefour), or Styrofoam pharmacy-style cool box.

How it works: No ice — just the insulation. Buffers temperature swings so a 25→38°C room translates to 27→32°C inside the container.

When to deploy: June through September, or anytime indoor temps consistently exceed 28°C.

Keep the original carton inside the insulated container, inside your coolest drawer.
Summer default
❄️
Tier 3 — Fridge during canicule only
Trigger: Forecast predicts >35°C indoors for 3+ consecutive days (typical canicule alert).

Action: Move ampoules to refrigerator (4°C) temporarily. Return to room temp storage when heatwave ends.

Warming protocol: Before drawing from a refrigerated ampoule, warm in your hand or shirt pocket for 20–30 minutes. Cold castor oil is too viscous to draw cleanly through filter needle.

NEVER: Use fridge as default storage. Only as a heat-emergency measure.
Emergency only
🍷
Optional — Wine fridge (one-time investment)
What: Small wine cooler set to 14–16°C.

Why ideal: Stays within manufacturer spec year-round. No viscosity issues. No crystallization risk. No seasonal switching protocol needed. No condensation cycling.

Cost: €100–200 one-time for a small unit (8–12 bottle capacity is more than enough).

When worth it: If you're committing to multi-year TRT and want zero storage worry. Optional, not necessary.
Premium option

SubQ injection technique — abdominal site

StepActionDetail
1Site selection5cm lateral to navel, alternate sides each injection
2Skin prepAlcohol swab in circular motion, allow to air dry 10 seconds
3Pinch fatPinch 2.5–5cm of subcutaneous fat between thumb and index finger
4Insert needle90° angle, fully insert the 12mm needle into pinched fat
5Inject slowlySteady pressure over 10–15 seconds (castor oil is viscous — slow push reduces tissue trauma)
6Pause5 seconds with needle in place before withdrawing (lets oil settle, prevents track leakage)
7WithdrawPull needle straight out, release pinch
8PressureBrief pressure with alcohol swab if any blood (minor bleeding is normal)
9DisposeEntire syringe + needle into DASRI sharps container
10LogRecord date, site, dose in tracking app or journal

Estradiol Management & Anastrozole Protocol

Why estradiol matters on TRT, how it's managed, and exactly when anastrozole is the right answer — versus when it would be a catastrophic mistake. Built around the central modern TRT principle: never prophylactic, always reactive to data + symptoms.

🚫 THE CARDINAL RULE: Never take anastrozole prophylactically. Crashed estradiol (<15 pg/mL) is worse than elevated estradiol. The TRT community learned this through years of unnecessary suffering caused by automatic anastrozole prescription. Modern protocol: measure E2, confirm symptoms, then consider AI — in that order, never reversed.

What estradiol is — and why men need it

⚗️
The hormone
Estradiol (E2) is the most potent form of estrogen. Despite its reputation as a "female hormone," men produce and require estradiol for many vital functions:

What E2 does for men:
• Bone density maintenance
• Libido and erectile function
• Cognitive function and mood
• Cardiovascular health
• Joint lubrication
• HDL cholesterol regulation
• Skin elasticity

Men with crashed E2 develop osteoporosis, joint pain, depression, and erectile dysfunction — even with normal testosterone.
Essential, not optional
🧬
How men produce E2
Men don't have ovaries — so we make E2 through a different pathway:

Aromatization: an enzyme called aromatase converts testosterone directly into estradiol.

Where aromatase lives:
• Adipose tissue (body fat) — primary site
• Testes (small contribution)
• Brain, bones, muscles (minor)

Key implication: more body fat = more aromatase = more T → E2 conversion. At your current BMI 35, aromatase activity is elevated. As you lose fat, the problem self-corrects.
Fat-dependent enzyme
🎯
Why TRT creates an E2 management question
When you inject testosterone, you're providing more substrate for aromatase. More T input → potentially more E2 output, depending on your individual aromatase activity.

The good news for your protocol:
• 167mg/week is moderate dose (vs 250mg+)
• SubQ route = lower peak T = less aromatization
• Twice-weekly frequency = smoother profile

Result: most men on your specific protocol don't need anastrozole. But your BMI 35 starting point means you're in the subset who might need it temporarily during the first months.
Manageable risk

Estradiol ranges — interpretation zones

E2 level (pg/mL)ZoneSymptoms expectedAction
<15🚨 CRASHED — DANGERSevere joint pain, ED, depression, fatigue, low libido, bone lossStop any AI immediately. Wait 2-4 weeks. May need temporary lower T dose.
15–20Low — symptomaticJoint stiffness, mood low, libido decliningReduce AI dose by half. Recheck in 4 weeks.
20–35✅ OPTIMAL — target zoneNone — feeling greatNo action. Continue protocol.
35–40Upper normal — watchUsually none, occasional mild water retentionNo action if asymptomatic. Recheck at next scheduled test.
40–50Elevated — context mattersPossible: water retention, sensitivity, mood variabilityIf asymptomatic → natural interventions first. If symptomatic → consider AI.
50–80High — needs actionWater retention, gynecomastia risk, mood instability, libido dropAnastrozole 0.25mg twice weekly + natural interventions

⚠️ LAB METHOD CRITICAL: Always request ultrasensitive estradiol (LC-MS/MS method) — standard immunoassays overestimate E2 in men by 30-100%. Standard assay showing E2 = 55 may actually be 30 on sensitive method. This single measurement error has caused millions of unnecessary anastrozole prescriptions. Always specify "estradiol ultrasensible" or "LC-MS/MS" when ordering.

High E2 symptoms — what to watch for

SystemSymptomMechanismSeverity

Chest (advanced)Visible breast tissue developing (gynecomastia)Permanent tissue growth if not addressed earlySurgical only after established

LibidoParadoxical low libido despite high TE2/T ratio imbalance — both ends of range cause low libidoMajor quality-of-life impact
CognitiveBrain fog, reduced motivation, "fuzzy" thinkingE2 excess affects dopamine signalingSubtle but persistent
CardiovascularBlood pressure elevation, possible palpitationsWater retention + vascular effectsMonitor BP if E2 elevated
SleepDisrupted sleep, vivid dreams, night sweatsHormonal imbalance affects sleep architectureCommon but reversible

Crashed E2 symptoms — why we never crash estradiol

SystemSymptomMechanismSeverity
JointsSevere joint pain, "dry bones" sensation, poppingE2 maintains joint lubrication and cartilageHallmark symptom — worst feature
LibidoTotal libido collapse, even worse than untreated low TE2 is essential for male libido — counterintuitive but establishedTotal quality of life destruction
Sexual functionErectile dysfunction unresponsive to PDE5 inhibitorsE2 required for vascular erectile functionFrustrating and often unattributed to AI
MoodDeep depression, anhedonia, anxietyE2 modulates serotonin and dopamineCan be severe enough to require psychiatric intervention
CognitiveBrain fog severe, memory issues, reduced motivationE2 critical for male cognitive functionAffects work performance
SkinDry skin, brittle nails, hair texture changesE2 essential for skin hydration and collagenVisible within weeks
EnergyProfound fatigue despite optimal T levelsE2 required for energy production pathwaysOften misattributed to "TRT not working"
BonesAccelerated bone density lossE2 is THE primary regulator of bone density in men tooSilent until fracture — long-term risk
CardiovascularHDL drops, lipid profile worsensE2 maintains favorable cardiovascular markersIncreases long-term CVD risk
🚨 RECOVERY FROM CRASHED E2: Takes 2-4 weeks after stopping anastrozole. The first week is the worst. Joint pain typically resolves last. Patients who crashed E2 often describe it as "the worst few weeks of my TRT journey" — entirely preventable mistake. This is why we never prescribe AI without measurement + symptoms.

💡 YOUR FAT LOSS IS THE PERMANENT SOLUTION: At BMI 35, you have elevated aromatase. At your target weight (72kg, BMI 23.5), aromatase activity drops 60-70%. Most TRT patients who needed AI at high body fat no longer need it once lean. The transformation protocol itself is the long-term E2 management strategy.

Anastrozole — what it is and how it works

💊
The medication
Class: Aromatase inhibitor (AI), third generation, non-steroidal
Brand name France: Arimidex (princeps), various generics
Form: 1mg tablets (scored for splitting)
Mechanism: Reversibly binds aromatase enzyme, blocking T→E2 conversion
Half-life: ~50 hours (long-acting)
Original indication: Post-menopausal breast cancer
TRT usage: Off-label, well-established in andrology practice

Cost: ~€20-30 per box of 28 tablets — lasts 3-7 months at TRT dose
Off-label use
⚖️
Dose sensitivity — critical concept
Anastrozole is extraordinarily potent at TRT-relevant doses. The breast cancer dose (1mg daily) is 14× the maximum recommended TRT dose. Implications:

• A pill-cutter creating quarters of 1mg tablets gives you the right dose
• Even 0.5mg twice weekly can crash E2 in many men
• Half-life of 50h means doses accumulate — patience is critical
• Effects continue for 2-4 weeks after stopping

Rule of thumb: Always start with the lowest possible dose. You can always increase. Decreasing requires waiting weeks for clearance.
Start low, go slow
🔬
Why anastrozole specifically (vs alternatives)
Anastrozole: most dose-titrable, shortest commitment, best for men. First choice.

Letrozole (Femara): 3x more potent than anastrozole. Too aggressive for TRT — easy to crash E2. Reserved for severe cases.

Exemestane (Aromasin): irreversible binder. Effect lasts longer. Less titratable. Used by some bodybuilders but not first-line for TRT.

Tamoxifen (Nolvadex): SERM not AI. Blocks E2 at receptors rather than reducing production. Used differently — for established gynecomastia, not routine E2 management.
Anastrozole = TRT standard

Anastrozole dosing protocol — titration approach

PhaseDoseFrequencyTimingDuration before reassessment
Starting dose0.25mg (quarter of 1mg tablet)Twice weeklyDay after each T injection (peak E2 day)6 weeks then retest E2
If E2 still elevated after 6 weeks0.5mg (half of 1mg tablet)Twice weeklyDay after T injection6 weeks then retest E2
If E2 crashed (<15) at any checkSTOP completelyN/A—Wait 4 weeks, recheck E2, restart at half dose if needed
If E2 perfect (20-35) and asymptomaticContinue current doseMaintainSame scheduleRecheck every 3 months with full panel
Maximum reasonable dose0.5mg twice weeklyAbove this = reassess T dose instead—If still high → reduce T dose or investigate
⚠️ TITRATION PRINCIPLE: Never jump doses. Each adjustment needs 6 weeks for full effect plus blood test for confirmation. Anastrozole accumulates and feedback takes weeks. Patients who "feel something" and add more pills crash their E2. Always wait for the blood test before adjusting.

Red flags — when to stop AI immediately

🦴
Joint pain develops
If you start experiencing joint pain (knees, ankles, fingers) within days/weeks of starting AI — this is the #1 sign of E2 dropping too low.

Action: Stop AI immediately. Test E2 within 1 week. Joint pain that develops on AI is rarely from other causes.
Stop AI immediately
😔
Mood crash or libido collapse
Sudden onset of depression, anhedonia, or libido disappearing while on AI = crashed E2 until proven otherwise.

Counterintuitive: low E2 destroys libido as much as low T. Many men attribute this to "TRT not working" when it's actually AI overdose.
Stop AI immediately
❌
ED unresponsive to PDE5 inhibitors
If you develop ED on AI and Cialis/Viagra don't work — this is classic crashed E2 ED. PDE5 inhibitors require some E2 to function properly.

Action: Stop AI. ED resolves within 2-4 weeks of E2 recovery.
Stop AI immediately
🌫️
Severe brain fog
Inability to focus, cognitive slowness, "fuzzy" thinking developing on AI = E2 too low.

E2 is essential for male cognitive function. Patients describe it as "thinking through cotton." Resolves with AI cessation.
Stop AI immediately

Common AI mistakes — what NOT to do

MistakeWhy it's wrongCorrect approach
Prophylactic AI from TRT day 1Most men don't need it. Crashes E2 in those who would have been fine.Wait for measurement + symptoms
Starting at 0.5mg or higherToo aggressive — likely to crash E2Always start at 0.25mg twice weekly
Adjusting dose by feel without retestSymptoms can be misleading; E2 takes weeks to stabilizeAlways retest before adjusting
Standard immunoassay for E2Overestimates E2 by 30-100% in men — leads to unnecessary AIAlways ultrasensitive LC-MS/MS method
Increasing dose if "feeling bloated"Water retention has many causes — not always E2Measure first, then decide
Daily dosingAnastrozole's 50h half-life makes daily dosing excessiveTwice weekly is standard maximum frequency
Continuing AI indefinitely "just in case"Body composition changes — needs evolve over monthsReassess every 3 months; stop if E2 well-controlled
Ignoring the E2/T ratioAbsolute E2 less important than ratio to TE2 35 with T 1000 may be fine; E2 35 with T 500 may be high

Discussing AI with your Geneva prescriber — talking points

Use these phrases to align with modern TRT best practice:

"Je préfère ne pas démarrer d'inhibiteur d'aromatase de manière prophylactique. Je préfère mesurer mon estradiol à 6 semaines avec une méthode ultrasensible (LC-MS/MS) et n'envisager l'anastrozole que si E2 est élevé ET symptomatique."

"Si l'anastrozole devient nécessaire, je voudrais commencer à dose très basse — 0.25mg deux fois par semaine — et titrer avec contrôle E2 à 6 semaines."

"Je suis conscient que l'estradiol bas est plus dangereux que l'estradiol élevé chez l'homme, et je veux éviter le scénario d'E2 effondré."

"Mon plan de perte de poids va réduire naturellement mon aromatase au fur et à mesure. L'objectif est de minimiser l'intervention médicamenteuse pendant cette transition."

🎯 SECTION SUMMARY: Estradiol is essential for male health — never the enemy. The job is to keep it in the optimal zone (20-35 pg/mL ultrasensitive method), not to suppress it. For your specific case: BMI 35 elevates aromatase, but your moderate TRT dose (167mg/week SubQ) minimizes the risk. Most men on your protocol don't need anastrozole. The transformation itself — fat loss — is the definitive long-term solution. If AI becomes needed, start at the lowest possible dose (0.25mg twice weekly), measure at 6 weeks, and never adjust without bloodwork. Crashed E2 is worse than elevated E2 — this is the single most important principle in modern TRT management. Your body needs estradiol to function. Respect it. 🧬

TRT Results Timeline + Training & Monitoring Adaptation

The complete 18-month roadmap — when each effect emerges, how to adapt resistance training at each phase to maximize results, and what to monitor to confirm the protocol is on track. This is your patience document — designed to be reread when motivation flags.

⚠️ THE FUNDAMENTAL TRUTH ABOUT TRT TIMELINE: TRT is not a steroid cycle. It brings your testosterone from low-normal to high-normal — not into bodybuilding territory. Visible results are evolution, not revolution. Men who get dramatic transformations show up consistently for 12-18 months while their peers quit at month 3 saying "it's not working." Stay the course.

The complete 18-month timeline at a glance

TimeframeWhat emergesWhat people noticeYour expected weight / BF%

Weeks 2–4Libido, energy, mood, sleep improvementsNothing visible~104kg / ~32%

Months 12–18Goal physique consolidation, refinementTarget physique achieved and sustained72–76kg / 10–13%

Phase 1 — Foundation (Months 0–3): establish the system

🎯
Internal experience
Week 1–2: Mood lift, motivation, mental clarity, vivid dreams. About 30–40% notice within first week.

Week 2–4: Libido becomes consistently elevated, morning erections return, energy stable at higher baseline, sleep quality often improves.

Week 4–6: Recovery faster between sessions, less DOMS, ability to push harder, possible 1–2kg water weight gain in muscles (NOT fat — intracellular hydration).

Month 2–3: Mirror shows subtle changes — arms slightly fuller, shoulders look squared, posture improves naturally.
Internal change > visible change
🏋️
Training adaptation
Focus: Establish base + grease the groove

Frequency: 5×/week (your current protocol)
Split: Upper / Lower / Push / Pull / Legs OR Upper x2 + Lower x2 + Full body
Volume: 12–16 working sets per muscle group per week
Intensity: 70–80% 1RM range, 8–12 reps mostly
Rest: 90 seconds compounds, 60s isolation

Critical move: Increase weight by 2.5kg/week on main compounds. Your recovery now exceeds your training stress for the first time in years. CAPTURE this with progressive overload.

Avoid: Joint-stressing exercises if you have knee history (Bulgarian split squats, deep loaded squats). Use machines and supported variations.
Progressive overload window
📊
Monitoring & milestones
Week 6 blood test: Total T, Free T, E2, Hematocrit. Confirm T in 700–1000 range. E2 should be 20–35 (target).

Month 3 blood test: Full panel.

Strength benchmarks Month 3:
• Compound lifts up 10–15% from baseline
• Bench press +5–10kg
• Deadlift / leg press +10–20kg
• Overhead press +2.5–5kg

Body benchmarks Month 3:
• Weight: 95–100kg
• Waist: −5cm from start
• Neck: +0.5cm
• Photos: subtle but real changes
First decision point

Phase 2 — Visible Results (Months 3–9): the recognition phase

👁️
External recognition
Month 3–6: Close family and friends notice. "Have you lost weight?" becomes a frequent question. Shoulders look broader, chest fuller, arms visibly bigger. Posture improves dramatically.

Month 6–9: Strangers begin asking what you've been doing. Clothing fits completely differently — shirts tighter on shoulders/chest, looser on waist. Face structure changes visibly — jaw line emerges as facial fat reduces.

Key visual changes: V-taper begins. Abdomen flatter though not yet defined. Upper chest, shoulders, arms lead the visible transformation. Lower body fat (love handles, lower belly) more stubborn.
Recognition phase
🏋️
Training adaptation
Focus: Hypertrophy + body composition

Frequency: 5×/week maintained
Split: Push/Pull/Legs × 6 days option, or Upper/Lower 4 days + 1 weak point day
Volume: Increase to 14–20 sets per muscle group/week
Intensity: Mix heavy (6–8 reps) + metabolic (12–20 reps)
Add: 2× HIIT cardio sessions/week post-training (6–8 rounds × 20s/40s)

Critical move: Begin tracking PRs systematically. This is when TRT-enabled gains become most visible in the numbers. Add weight to main lifts every 2 weeks minimum.

Add weak point work: Identify lagging areas (often rear delts, upper chest, calves) and add direct stimulus 2× weekly.
Maximum gains window
📊
Monitoring & milestones
Month 6 blood test: Full panel + DEXA scan if accessible.

Strength benchmarks Month 6:
• 20–25% strength increase from baseline
• Bench: +10–20kg
• Deadlift: +20–40kg
• OHP: +5–10kg
• Pull-ups: +3–5 reps

Body benchmarks Month 6:
• Weight: 88–95kg
• Body fat: 22–26%
• Waist: −10cm from start
• Chest measurement: maintained or +1cm despite fat loss
• Photos vs Month 0: dramatic difference visible
Major reassessment

Phase 3 — Transformation (Months 9–18): refinement to target physique

🏆
The polish phase
Month 9–12: Upper abdominal definition emerges. V-taper fully visible. Face dramatically changed — jaw line sharp, double chin gone. Strangers asking for advice. Clothes shopping becomes a new experience.

Month 12–18: Lower abdominal definition appears (the last to come). Refined, sustainable lean physique. Skin tightens around new composition. Body composition fundamentally transformed.

Critical understanding: The closer to 10% you get, the harder each percentage becomes. Going from 17% to 13% is roughly equal effort to going from 13% to 10%. The last phase requires the most precision.
Diminishing returns increase precision needs
🏋️
Training adaptation
Focus: Recomp + refinement

Frequency: 5–6×/week
Split: PPL × 6 days or upper/lower 5 days with weak point emphasis
Volume: 16–22 sets per muscle group/week
Intensity: Wave periodization — 3 heavy weeks / 1 deload
Cardio: Increase HIIT to 3×/week + 1 LISS session (60–90min walking)

Critical move: Shift from "adding weight" to "perfect execution." Strength gains slow naturally as you lean out. Focus on form, mind-muscle connection, time under tension, and weak point correction.

Add diet breaks: Every 8 weeks, 2 weeks at maintenance calories. Reduces cortisol, restores leptin, improves hormonal environment for continued progress.
Refinement > growth
📊
Monitoring & milestones
Quarterly blood tests: Maintain full panel. Watch for hormonal adaptation (TSH drift, cortisol elevation from chronic deficit).

Strength benchmarks Month 12:
• Strength plateauing as body fat drops (NORMAL)
• Focus on maintaining lifts as weight drops — preservation = win
• Bench: 1.2× bodyweight goal
• Deadlift: 2× bodyweight goal
• OHP: 0.7× bodyweight goal

Body benchmarks Month 12:
• Weight: 76–82kg
• Body fat: 13–17%
• Visible upper abdominals
• Defined arms, chest, shoulders
• Target physique within sight
Approaching goal

Why TRT changes how you should train — the physiology

🔬
Recovery capacity expanded
Optimal T levels mean nitrogen retention, glycogen replenishment, and protein synthesis all run at higher capacity. Your body can handle and benefit from 30–50% more training volume than at low-normal T.

Action: Add 2–4 working sets per major muscle group between Month 3 and Month 9. The volume that overtrained you before now becomes productive stimulus.
Recovery enables more work
💪
Strength gains accelerate
T directly affects neural drive AND muscle protein synthesis. Expect strength to increase faster than at any prior point in life — provided you progressively overload.

Action: Aggressive but smart progression. Add weight when you complete all sets/reps cleanly. Don't waste this window — it lasts roughly 6–12 months at maximum responsiveness.
Capture the window
🧬
Muscle protein synthesis elevated
The anabolic signal from each training session is amplified at optimal T. Even moderate stimulus creates more growth response.

Action: Train harder, but also eat protein more strategically. Spread your 130–160g protein across 4 meals rather than 2. The MPS-stimulating effect of a meal lasts ~5h; multiple meals stack the anabolic response.
Maximize anabolic signal
🩹
Injury risk paradox
T accelerates tendon repair AND increases strength gains. Problem: tendons strengthen slower than muscles. You can suddenly lift more than your tendons are conditioned for.

Action: Don't ego-lift in months 3–9. Form discipline more important than ever. Especially given your knee history — avoid the temptation to test new strength on heavy single-leg work.
Tendons lag muscle gains

What slows the timeline vs what accelerates it

⛔
Slows the timeline
Sleep deficit: Less than 7h destroys T production, raises cortisol, impairs recovery. Single biggest accelerator/decelerator.

Inconsistent training: 3 sessions one week, 5 the next = chaos signal to body. Consistency > intensity.

Diet breaks/cheat days: Mounjaro + carnivore is metabolically gentle; cheat meals introduce metabolic chaos.

Chronic stress: Cortisol antagonizes T. High-stress periods slow visible progress dramatically.

Hidden E2 elevation: Water retention masks muscle definition. The mirror lies when E2 is high.

Suboptimal protein timing: All protein in 1–2 meals leaves anabolic windows unused.

Excessive caloric deficit: >500 kcal deficit/day suppresses T, slows metabolism, burns muscle.
Avoid these patterns
🚀
Accelerates the timeline
Sleep 8+ hours consistently: Peak T production happens during deep sleep. This is non-negotiable.

Aggressive progressive overload: Capture the TRT recovery window with weekly weight increases.

Protein spread across 4 meals: Multiple MPS spikes daily > one big meal.

Stable, low stress environment: Manage cortisol via meditation, ashwagandha, sauna, cold.

Optimal E2 in target zone: 25–35 pg/mL — neither high nor crashed.

Sauna + cold plunge consistency: Recovery acceleration tools — keep daily.

Hydration + electrolytes: 3L water + 5g sodium + electrolytes daily on carnivore.

Strict protocol adherence: Every element matters. 95% adherence beats 100% intensity at 60% adherence.
Do these consistently

Honest caveats — the realism check

Honest truthImplication
TRT is not a steroid cycleBrings T from low-normal to high-normal — not bodybuilder territory. Results are evolution.
Mounjaro + carnivore are doing more visible work than TRT in first 6 monthsTRT's job is protecting muscle during this fat loss. The visible transformation is mostly your other protocol elements.
Individual response varies 15-20%Some men respond faster, some slower. Your timeline may differ from averages.
Lower body fat (love handles, lower abs) responds lastDon't measure progress only by lower abs. Look at the whole physique.
Strength gains slow as body fat dropsMaintaining lifts at lower bodyweight = winning, not stagnating.
Marketing photos are misleadingDramatic transformations in 3 months usually involve: years of prior training, undisclosed anabolics, lighting/posing tricks. Real timeline = 12–18 months.
Plateaus are inevitable3–4 plateau periods over 18 months is normal. Each requires protocol adjustment, not abandonment.
The closer to 10%, the harder each % becomes17% → 13% requires similar effort to 13% → 10%. Plan accordingly.

Peptides — Where They Fit (and Don't) in Your Stack

A map of the peptide landscape relative to what you already run — Mounjaro for fat loss, TRT for hormones, plus training, protein, and thermal therapy. The honest throughline: peptides are the last 10–20% of polish, not the first 80% of results. This section explains what each class actually does, how strong the evidence is, and how it would interact with your specific protocol — so the decision is informed, not hype-driven.

⚠️ READ FIRST: Most peptides below (except Tesamorelin) are unregulated research chemicals with variable evidence and no quality guarantees. None replace the fundamentals. Any use of injectable peptides is a medical decision that requires a qualified physician and your current bloodwork — especially given your borderline hematocrit (50.3%) and the fact you already stack two hormone-active agents. This section is an educational reference only: it intentionally contains no doses, schedules, or sourcing.

Real-world impact hierarchy — body recomposition for a 40-year-old already on Mounjaro

ToolPrimary leverReal-world magnitudeEvidenceIn your context
Testosterone (TRT)Muscle protein synthesis, strength, muscle retention in a deficitLarge (if T is low)Strong in hypogonadismReplacement if labs are low; enhancement if normal — very different risk/benefit
Mounjaro (tirzepatide)Appetite ↓, fat loss, insulin sensitivityLarge for fat lossStrong (RCTs)Already your fat-loss engine — most "peptide for fat loss" goals are already covered
Training + protein + sleepHypertrophy, muscle retentionLarge — foundationalStrongThe 80%. Protein ~1.6–2.2 g/kg, progressive lifting, 7h+ sleep. Non-negotiable base
GH secretagogues
CJC-1295, Ipamorelin, Sermorelin		↑ pulsatile GH / IGF-1Modest — recovery, sleep, connective tissue; weak muscle builderVariable / weak in healthy adultsOptional polish; can nudge glucose and insulin the wrong way for your GKI goals
TesamorelinGHRH analog targeting visceral fatModerate for visceral fat specificallyStrongest of the GH peptides (approved for HIV lipodystrophy)The one with real visceral-fat data — but your visceral fat (13) is already trending down on the current stack
Healing peptides
BPC-157, TB-500		Tissue / tendon / gut repairUnclear in humansMostly animal data; unapprovedInjury-recovery interest (e.g. your hand) — not a body-composition tool

The classes, explained

🧬
GH secretagogues — CJC-1295 · Ipamorelin · Sermorelin
What they do: prompt your own pituitary to release growth hormone in pulses, raising IGF-1 — rather than injecting GH directly.

Realistic benefits: deeper sleep, faster recovery, connective-tissue support, some visceral-fat mobilization.

Reality check: far weaker for muscle than testosterone; often produces more water retention than lean tissue; evidence in healthy, non-deficient adults is thin.

Watch for you: GH activity can reduce insulin sensitivity and raise fasting glucose — directly working against your ketosis and complicating Mounjaro's glucose effect. WADA-prohibited; research-chemical status.
Polish, not foundation
🩺
Tesamorelin — the evidence outlier
What it is: a GHRH analog and the one GH-axis peptide with genuine clinical data — FDA-approved (as Egrifta) to reduce visceral adipose tissue in a specific patient group.

Why it stands apart: of all the GH peptides, it has the best-quality evidence for visceral fat reduction specifically.

For you: prescription-grade and costly, and your visceral fat is already responding to Mounjaro + carnivore + thermal. It's the rational first stop if a physician ever judged visceral fat the limiting factor — but you're not there.
Real data · not currently needed
🩹
Healing peptides — BPC-157 · TB-500
What they're marketed for: tendon, ligament, muscle, gut and wound healing.

Evidence: overwhelmingly animal / rodent; robust human trials are largely absent. Both are unapproved and WADA-prohibited.

Relevance to you: the interest here is injury recovery (your hand surgery), not body composition — and even for recovery the human evidence is weak. Standard rehab, protein, and sleep remain the proven levers.
Recovery claims · weak human evidence

Why testosterone and GH are "complementary" — and what that really means

They act through different pathways, which is why physique athletes historically paired them. Testosterone drives myofibrillar hypertrophy — actual contractile muscle — and is the single most powerful muscle hormone. GH / IGF-1 supports connective tissue, recovery, nutrient partitioning, and fat mobilization, but is a relatively poor muscle builder.

Analogy: testosterone is the construction crew that builds the house; GH is the logistics-and-maintenance crew that helps them work and recover. Together they can exceed either alone — but the builders do most of the work.

For your hierarchy: this is exactly why TRT (if your labs justify replacement) sits far above any GH peptide. The peptides are the support crew, not the build.

Flags specific to YOUR stack

FlagWhy it matters for youAction
HematocritTRT already raises it; your 50.3% is borderline. GH fluid shifts add noise.Keep <52%, donate before TRT start, recheck every 6 weeks
Glucose / GKI / ketosisGH and secretagogues can blunt insulin sensitivity and raise fasting glucose — against your ketosis and your Mounjaro glucose effect.Never layer GH peptides without watching GKI closely
Total physiological loadYou already stack 18:6 fasting + sauna + cold + 5×/week training + two hormone-active agents. Each addition has a recovery cost.Add one variable at a time; never during instability (e.g. after a hypo)
Fertility (TRT)TRT suppresses LH/FSH and can impair fertility — relevant given your son and any future family plans.Decide before starting; ask a physician about fertility-sparing options

The honest decision framework — in order

#StepWhy it comes first
1Full morning hormone panel: total T, free T, SHBG, LH, FSH, estradiol, IGF-1 + hematocritYou can't evaluate TRT or any peptide without baseline numbers (see your Bloodwork sections)
2Establish replacement vs enhancementLow T + symptoms + labs → TRT is replacement (high value). Normal T → enhancement (smaller benefit, lifelong commitment)
3Max out the free 80%: protein, progressive training, sleep, recoveryMounjaro already handles fat loss; these protect muscle. Most "peptide" goals are met here
4Only then consider peptides — with a physician holding your labsTesamorelin is the only one with solid clinical evidence, mainly for visceral fat you're already reducing
🎯 BOTTOM LINE: For a 40-year-old already on Mounjaro with a dialled-in training, protein, and thermal stack, the highest-leverage hormone decision is TRT done properly off real labs — not GH peptides. GH secretagogues are recovery-and-sleep polish with weak muscle payoff and a glucose downside that fights your ketosis; healing peptides are recovery experiments with thin human evidence; Tesamorelin is the one with real data but addresses visceral fat you're already losing. Peptides are the last 10–20%, not the first 80%. Any use of unprescribed injectables belongs in a conversation with a qualified physician who has your bloodwork in front of them. This section is a map — by design it carries no doses, schedules, or sourcing.

~18:30-19:30First meal (breaking fast)D3 5000 IU + K2 100mcg · Boron 10mg · Selenium 200mcg · Zinc 30mg · Omega-3 3-4g · Enzyme complexSalt on foodAll fat-soluble + food-dependent supplements here · highest absorption window · NO electrolyte tablets (zinc/calcium competition) · monitoring D3 sleep impact
19:30-22:00Continuous grazingPlain water · food—No supplements · just food

~23:00-23:30Pre-bed (30-60 min before sleep)Magnesium bisglycinate 400mg + Glycine 3-5g + L-Theanine 200mg + Ashwagandha 600mg—Sleep optimization stack · 4 supplements together for synergy
24:00Sleep——Eating window closes · 18h fast begins
💡 KEY SYNERGIES: Magnesium + Glycine + L-Theanine + Ashwagandha taken together = powerful sleep stack (temperature drop, alpha waves, GABA, cortisol drop). D3 + K2 must always be together (K2 directs calcium to bones). Boron + Zinc both lower SHBG (raise free T). Omega-3 needs dietary fat — carnivore meals are ideal vehicle. L-Carnitine fasted = better absorption (no protein competition).

🧪 7-day phased elimination test (4-11 June 2026)

📅
The test
What changes: Vit4ever powder eliminated · all else maintained

What stays: Canderel (3 coffees/day) · flavored creatine (10g workout water) · all other supplements unchanged

What's measured: Daily morning GKI before cold plunge

Why phased: Tests dominant variable first (Vit4ever = ~65% of total sucralose) with minimal lifestyle disruption.
Active 4-11 June
🎯
Decision tree at Day 7
GKI <3.0 → Vit4ever was the bottleneck. Current setup sufficient long-term. Maintain Canderel + flavored creatine.

GKI 3.0-4.0 → Significant improvement. Optional next step: switch creatine to unflavored.

GKI >4.0 → Test inconclusive. Eliminate Canderel + creatine for next phase. Also investigate other factors.
Wed 11 June
📊
Measurement protocol
Daily routine:
1. Wake, bathroom
2. Rest 5 minutes
3. Glucose finger prick (mg/dL)
4. Ketones finger prick (mmol/L)
5. Calculate GKI = (glucose ÷ 18) ÷ ketones
6. THEN cold plunge

Same time each day · before cold to avoid cortisol elevation
Daily

📋 PRODUCTS TO CHECK NEXT: Verify your collagen powder label (30g/day - significant volume). Also verify any other powdered supplements. Capsule supplements are typically safe but check anyway. The word "édulcorant" or any "E9xx" number in 950-969 range = artificial sweetener confirmed.
🎯 PROTOCOL SUMMARY: Your supplement stack covers all 6 protocol pillars (hormone optimization, sleep, recovery, training, hydration, ketosis support). Total active supplements: 18. Daily timing organized into 4 distinct windows (06:30 / 15:30-15:45 / 18:30 / 23:00). Critical sucralose audit identified 3 sources — phased elimination test underway. By following this timing protocol with discipline, you maximize the bioavailability and synergistic effects of every supplement while avoiding the timing errors that blunt their benefits. Update this section as you add or change supplements. 💊
27

Resources Library Books & Knowledge for Lifelong Optimization

A curated library to deepen your understanding of every mechanism that intervenes in your protocol — from hormones to nutrition, training to recovery, biochemistry to longevity. Each entry includes what you'll learn and the core concepts. Build agency through knowledge: the more you understand your body, the better decisions you make over the next 30+ years.

📚 HOW TO USE THIS LIBRARY: Don't try to read everything at once. Start with 1 book per protocol pillar most relevant to your current phase. Foundation reading order suggestion: (1) Why We Get Sick (Bikman) for metabolic foundations, (2) The Carnivore Code (Saladino) for diet rationale, (3) Why We Sleep (Walker) for recovery, (4) The Testosterone Optimization Therapy Bible (Rouzier) for hormone phase. Then branch based on interest.

🔬 Hormone optimization & TRT

📕
The Testosterone Optimization Therapy Bible
Author: Jay Campbell (Foreword by Dr. Keith Nichols)

In a nutshell: The most practical, comprehensive guide to TRT from a patient-advocate perspective. Covers dosing protocols, injection techniques, ester selection, side effect management, ancillaries (AI, hCG), bloodwork interpretation, and lifestyle optimization.

Core concepts:
• Why most doctors get TRT wrong (under-dosing, weekly vs more frequent dosing)
• Hematocrit management without donating blood
• SubQ vs IM injection comparison
• Estradiol management and the "crashed E2" risk
• Free T vs Total T — what matters
• How to interpret bloodwork beyond reference ranges
• SHBG and its impact on free hormone levels

Why critical for you: Directly applicable as you prepare for TRT initiation post-hematocrit normalization.
Must-read for TRT
📕
Testosterone: A Man's Guide
Author: Nelson Vergel

In a nutshell: Patient-centered guide to androgen therapy with emphasis on practical management. Vergel runs ExcelMale forum and brings 30+ years of patient advocacy experience.

Core concepts:
• Symptom-based vs lab-based diagnosis
• The "syndrome of low T" — beyond just numbers
• Anastrozole protocols and when NOT to use
• Managing TRT through travel, illness, life changes
• Fertility preservation strategies (hCG)
• Long-term cardiovascular safety of TRT
• Cycling vs continuous therapy debates

Why useful: Complements Campbell's book with deeper patient-experience perspective.
Practical TRT
📕
The Hormone Handbook
Author: Dr. Thierry Hertoghe

In a nutshell: Belgian endocrinologist's comprehensive guide to bioidentical hormone replacement covering testosterone, growth hormone, thyroid, cortisol, DHEA, melatonin. European functional medicine perspective.

Core concepts:
• Multi-hormone optimization (not just T)
• Thyroid optimization (relevant for your TSH 3.58)
• Growth hormone deficiency in adults
• Cortisol rhythm restoration
• DHEA and pregnenolone basics
• European functional medicine philosophy
• Why "normal" reference ranges miss optimal

Why useful for you: Hertoghe practices in Brussels — accessible to you in Geneva area. His framework helpful for finding similar practitioners.
European perspective

🥩 Nutrition · carnivore · keto · fasting

📗
The Carnivore Code
Author: Dr. Paul Saladino, MD

In a nutshell: The most rigorous scientific case for carnivore eating. Saladino challenges plant-food orthodoxy with evolutionary biology, biochemistry, and clinical experience.

Core concepts:
• Plant defense chemicals (lectins, oxalates, phytates, glucosinolates)
• Why "fiber is essential" is a myth
• Nutrient density: animal vs plant foods
• Cholesterol myths and cardiovascular truth
• Saturated fat — biochemistry vs dogma
• Nose-to-tail eating and organ meats
• Why the carnivore diet works for autoimmunity
• Practical implementation strategies

Why critical: Foundational rationale for your current diet. Note: Saladino has since moderated his position to include some fruit/honey — worth reading both his original and newer work.
Diet foundation
📗
The Carnivore Diet
Author: Dr. Shawn Baker

In a nutshell: The athlete-orthopedic surgeon who popularized modern carnivore. Less scientific than Saladino but more practical and motivational.

Core concepts:
• Personal transformation story
• Performance and recovery on carnivore
• Practical "just eat meat" simplicity
• Autoimmune disease reversal cases
• Athletic performance evidence
• Common mistakes beginners make
• Why complications often resolve unexpectedly

Why useful: Practical companion to Saladino's deeper science. Baker has trained thousands of carnivore practitioners.
Practical guide
📗
Why We Get Sick
Author: Dr. Benjamin Bikman

In a nutshell: The single most important book for understanding insulin resistance — the root mechanism of metabolic disease. Bikman is a leading insulin researcher.

Core concepts:
• Insulin resistance as root cause of most chronic disease
• How insulin regulates fat storage vs burning
• Why "calories in, calories out" is incomplete
• Insulin's role in Alzheimer's (type 3 diabetes)
• Cancer-insulin connection
• PCOS, infertility, and insulin
• How to measure insulin resistance properly
• Dietary strategies to restore sensitivity

Why essential: Explains WHY your protocol works at the cellular level. Reading this transforms how you understand metabolism.
Metabolic foundation
📗
The Complete Guide to Fasting
Author: Dr. Jason Fung & Jimmy Moore

In a nutshell: The definitive practical guide to intermittent and extended fasting. Fung is a nephrologist who pioneered therapeutic fasting for type 2 diabetes.

Core concepts:
• Why fasting works (insulin reduction primary)
• Different fasting protocols (16:8, 18:6, 24h, 36h, 72h+)
• Hormonal changes during fasting
• Autophagy and cellular renewal
• Breaking fasts safely
• Fasting for weight loss vs longevity
• Common mistakes and how to avoid
• When NOT to fast

Why critical for you: You're doing 18:6 daily — understand exactly what's happening in your body during your 18-hour fast.
Fasting science
📗
The Obesity Code
Author: Dr. Jason Fung

In a nutshell: Fung's earlier work establishing why conventional weight loss advice fails. Required reading for understanding metabolic resistance.

Core concepts:
• Insulin as the primary regulator of fat
• Why "eat less, move more" fails long-term
• Set point theory and how to change it
• The role of carb timing and frequency
• Cortisol's role in obesity
• Why diets fail (metabolic adaptation)
• Hormonal vs caloric models of obesity

Why important: Understanding obesity's true mechanism prevents falling back to "just eat less" thinking when your protocol challenges that paradigm.
Obesity science
📗
Good Calories, Bad Calories
Author: Gary Taubes

In a nutshell: Investigative science journalism dismantling the diet-heart hypothesis. Taubes traces how nutritional dogma diverged from actual science over 100 years.

Core concepts:
• The history of dietary fat demonization
• Ancel Keys and the Seven Countries Study fraud
• Carbohydrate-insulin model of obesity
• Why epidemiology can't establish causation
• The flaws in dietary guidelines
• How institutions reinforce wrong ideas
• The actual evidence on saturated fat

Why fundamental: Builds healthy skepticism about mainstream nutrition advice. Once you read this, you can never trust "low-fat is healthy" guidelines again.
Critical thinking
