Mighty Mojo: GLP-1 Medications
Author: Dr. Tyna Moore (also extensive podcast content)

In a nutshell: One of few clinician resources on optimizing GLP-1 therapy beyond weight loss — focused on muscle preservation, training, and long-term protocol design.

Core concepts:
• Why most patients lose muscle on GLP-1s (and how to prevent)
• Microdosing protocols and rationale
• Combining GLP-1s with resistance training
• Protein requirements during GLP-1 therapy
• Exit strategies from GLP-1s
• GLP-1s for longevity beyond weight loss
• Side effect management protocols

Why critical for you: You're on Mounjaro and training intensely — this addresses your exact use case. Tyna Moore podcast episodes also valuable.
Mounjaro-specific
📘
Outlive
Author: Dr. Peter Attia

In a nutshell: The current gold standard book on longevity medicine. Attia provides a complete framework for "Medicine 3.0" — proactive prevention of the diseases that will kill most people.

Core concepts:
• The "4 horsemen" (cardiovascular, cancer, neurodegeneration, metabolic disease)
• The "centenarian decathlon" framework
• Apolipoprotein B (ApoB) as the true cardiovascular marker
• Zone 2 cardio (mitochondrial training)
• Strength training for longevity
• Sleep as the cornerstone
• The case for proactive testing
• Medication strategies for prevention
• Emotional health as longevity factor

Why essential: Provides the long-term framework beyond just transformation. Read this once you've stabilized your current protocol.
Longevity framework

🏋️ Exercise physiology · training · performance

📙
Bigger Leaner Stronger
Author: Mike Matthews

In a nutshell: Evidence-based, no-nonsense guide to body recomposition through resistance training. Cuts through bro-science with actual research citations.

Core concepts:
• Progressive overload as primary driver
• Compound vs isolation exercises
• Optimal volume, frequency, intensity
• Macronutrient targets for recomp
• Why high-protein is critical
• Periodization for natural lifters
• Common mistakes and plateaus
• Cardio's role (limited, strategic)

Why useful: Solid evidence-based training framework. Matthews also has detailed online resources at Legion Athletics.
Training basics
📙
Starting Strength
Author: Mark Rippetoe

In a nutshell: The definitive guide to barbell training fundamentals. Detailed technical breakdown of squat, deadlift, bench press, overhead press, and power clean.

Core concepts:
• Why barbell compound movements > everything else
• Exact mechanics of the big lifts
• Linear progression for novices
• Why "form first" is the only sustainable approach
• The role of the hip drive
• Bar path principles
• Common errors and corrections
• Why strength is the foundation skill

Why fundamental: If you ever want to add compound lifts to your training, this is the only book you need. Rippetoe's approach has shaped a generation of lifters.
Technical mastery
📙
Science and Practice of Strength Training
Author: Vladimir Zatsiorsky & William Kraemer

In a nutshell: Graduate-level textbook on strength training science. Zatsiorsky was the Soviet sport science legend who trained Olympic weightlifters.

Core concepts:
• Neural vs muscular adaptations
• Force-velocity-power relationships
• Periodization theory and practice
• Recovery and overtraining
• Maximum effort vs dynamic effort vs repetition methods
• Sport-specific training
• The science behind program design
• Rate coding and motor unit recruitment

Why advanced: For when you want to truly understand the deep science of strength. Not beginner-friendly but unmatched depth.
Advanced science

In a nutshell: The most important book on sleep science. UC Berkeley sleep researcher distills 30+ years of research into actionable insights.

Core concepts:
• Why sleep is non-negotiable (not optional)
• Sleep stages and their specific functions
• REM vs deep sleep (different purposes)
• Sleep and memory consolidation
• Sleep and emotional regulation
• Sleep and immune function
• Sleep deprivation's true cost
• The brain's glymphatic cleansing during sleep
• Caffeine's true half-life (longer than you think)
• Alcohol's destruction of sleep architecture

Why critical: Sleep is the cornerstone of everything else in your protocol. Without quality sleep, hormones don't optimize, body doesn't recover, muscles don't grow.
Sleep foundation

• The Wim Hof breathing method

• Mental commitment as the third pillar

• Cold and immune function
• Practical progression for cold exposure
• Safety considerations

Cold exposure

Stress management

🔥❄️ Sauna · cold · contrast therapy · HSP & hormesis

The science of deliberate heat and cold exposure — heat shock proteins, brown adipose tissue, hormetic stress, growth hormone optimization, and contrast therapy mechanisms. Honest disclosure: dedicated popular books on sauna science are rare. Most quality content is in research papers, podcasts, and chapters within broader books.

❄️
Winter Swimming: The Nordic Way Towards a Healthier and Happier Life
Author: Dr. Susanna Søberg

In a nutshell: Danish researcher's evidence-based exploration of cold and contrast therapy. Søberg's research established the "end on cold" principle and quantified contrast therapy benefits.

Core concepts:
• Brown adipose tissue (BAT) activation by cold
• Cold shock proteins (RBM3, CIRP)
• Why ending cold matters (vasoconstriction = anti-inflammatory)
• Contrast therapy protocols (sauna + cold rounds)
• Nordic vs Finnish traditions
• Mental resilience from cold exposure
• The Søberg Principle: always end cold
• Practical progression for beginners
• Sex differences in cold response

Why critical for you: Directly relevant to your new contrast therapy protocol. Søberg's research is THE evidence base for the protocol you just designed.
Contrast therapy essential
🔥
The Sauna Book
Author: Tom Johnson & Tim Miller

In a nutshell: Classic comprehensive guide to traditional Finnish sauna — history, culture, construction, and health benefits. The most thorough single-volume sauna resource.

Core concepts:
• Traditional Finnish sauna design and use
• Optimal sauna temperatures and durations
• Löyly (steam) practices
• Cultural and historical context
• Construction principles
• Health benefits documented
• Different sauna types comparison

Limitation: Older book, lighter on modern research. But comprehensive on practice.

Why useful: Foundation knowledge of sauna tradition and proper practice.
Practice foundation
🧬
Hormesis in Health and Disease
Editors: Suresh Rattan & Éric Le Bourg (academic textbook)

In a nutshell: Academic textbook on hormesis — the principle that low-dose stressors cause beneficial adaptations. The scientific framework underlying sauna, cold, fasting, and exercise benefits.

Core concepts:
• Hormesis dose-response curves
• Heat shock proteins (HSP70, HSP90, HSF1)
• Cold shock proteins (RBM3, CIRP)
• Mitochondrial hormesis (mitohormesis)
• Exercise as hormesis
• Caloric restriction as hormesis
• Why "what doesn't kill you makes you stronger" is true biology
• The U-shaped dose-response
• Hormetic interventions for longevity

Why valuable: Academic depth on the mechanism behind sauna, cold, fasting, and training. For when you want the deep science.

Best used as: Reference for specific hormetic mechanisms. Not casual reading.
Academic reference
🌡️
Heat Shock Proteins and Whole Body Physiology
Editor: Alexzander A. A. Asea & Bukhtiar H. Shah (academic)

In a nutshell: Academic compilation specifically on HSPs and their physiological roles. The deepest single source on heat shock proteins.

Core concepts:
• HSP70 structure and function
• HSP90 in muscle and metabolism
• HSF1 (heat shock factor) signaling
• HSPs in exercise adaptation
• HSPs in muscle preservation
• Heat acclimatization timeline
• Sauna protocols for HSP induction
• HSPs in aging and longevity
• Therapeutic applications

Best used as: Look-up source for specific HSP questions. Heavy science, not casual reading.

Why useful: Most authoritative HSP-specific source. Free chapters often available via PubMed.
HSP reference
📰
Sauna bathing is associated with reduced risk... (Laukkanen et al.)
Type: Landmark research paper (2015, JAMA Internal Medicine)

In a nutshell: The famous Finnish study of 2,315 men over 20 years that established sauna's cardiovascular and all-cause mortality benefits. Search PubMed for free access to abstract.

Core findings:
• 4-7 sauna sessions/week → 40% reduction in all-cause mortality
• 50% reduction in cardiovascular mortality
• 66% reduction in dementia
• Dose-response relationship (more sauna = more benefit)
• Optimal session: 19+ minutes at 80°C+
• Independent of other factors (exercise, smoking, etc.)

Caveat for you: Used traditional Finnish sauna (80°C+ with head heated). Your head-out portable likely delivers 60-80% of these benefits at most.

Why read: The most-cited sauna study. Establishes the evidence base.
Landmark study
📰
Cardiovascular and Other Health Benefits of Sauna Bathing
Authors: Laukkanen, Laukkanen & Kunutsor (review paper)

In a nutshell: Comprehensive review of all sauna health research as of 2018. Free on PubMed. The single best summary of sauna science available.

Core concepts covered:
• All cardiovascular benefits with mechanisms
• Endothelial function improvements
• Blood pressure effects
• Heart rate variability
• Inflammation reduction
• Growth hormone release (1.6× elevation, multiple-fold with stacking)
• Cognitive benefits and BDNF
• Optimal protocols
• Safety considerations
• Mechanisms behind benefits

Why critical: If you read ONE thing on sauna science, read this review paper (free).
Review paper · free

🧬 Human body · biochemistry · cellular mechanisms

Foundational understanding of how your body works at every scale — from molecules to organs. These books teach you to reason from first principles about every intervention in your protocol.

📔
Lehninger Principles of Biochemistry
Author: David Nelson & Michael Cox

In a nutshell: THE graduate biochemistry textbook. Covers every metabolic pathway, enzyme system, and biochemical mechanism in the human body. Reference for life.

Core concepts:
• Glycolysis, Krebs cycle, electron transport chain
• Beta-oxidation of fatty acids
• Ketogenesis and ketone metabolism
• Gluconeogenesis and the Cori cycle
• Protein synthesis and amino acid metabolism
• Hormone signaling cascades
• Enzyme kinetics and regulation
• All vitamin and mineral cofactor roles

Best used as: Reference book. Look up specific topics as questions arise. Not for cover-to-cover reading.
Reference
📔
Guyton and Hall Textbook of Medical Physiology
Author: John Hall

In a nutshell: The standard medical physiology textbook used worldwide. Covers every body system and how they integrate. Accessible explanations of complex topics.

Core concepts:
• Cardiovascular physiology (BP, cardiac output)
• Renal function and acid-base balance
• Endocrine system mechanisms
• Nervous system organization
• Digestive physiology
• Muscle physiology and energetics
• Temperature regulation
• Integration of all systems

Best used as: Read chapters relevant to your interests. Cardiovascular section especially relevant given your BP focus.
Reference
📔
Molecular Biology of the Cell
Author: Bruce Alberts et al.

In a nutshell: The most comprehensive cell biology textbook. Covers every cellular process from DNA to organelles to cellular communication.

Core concepts:
• Cell membrane structure and signaling
• Mitochondrial biogenesis and function
• Autophagy mechanisms in detail
• Protein folding and degradation
• Cell cycle and apoptosis
• Stem cells and tissue renewal
• Intercellular communication
• Cancer biology basics

Best used as: Deep dive when you want to understand HOW cells actually work. Beautifully illustrated, makes complex concepts accessible.
Reference
📔
Power, Sex, Suicide: Mitochondria and the Meaning of Life
Author: Nick Lane

In a nutshell: Brilliant accessible exploration of mitochondria — the energy-producing organelles whose health determines metabolism, aging, and disease. Reads like a science thriller.

Core concepts:
• Mitochondrial endosymbiotic origin
• ATP production via oxidative phosphorylation
• Why mitochondria have their own DNA
• Free radicals and aging (oxidative stress)
• Mitochondrial dysfunction in chronic disease
• How exercise builds new mitochondria
• Cold exposure and mitochondrial biogenesis
• Apoptosis (programmed cell death)
• The mitochondrial theory of aging

Why essential: Mitochondria are the central organ of metabolism. Training, cold exposure, fasting, ketones — all converge on mitochondrial function.
Mitochondria
📔
Transformer: The Deep Chemistry of Life and Death
Author: Nick Lane

In a nutshell: Lane's masterwork on the Krebs cycle — the central metabolic pathway connecting all energy production. Reveals how the Krebs cycle runs forward AND backward depending on cellular state.

Core concepts:
• Why the Krebs cycle is at the center of metabolism
• Reverse Krebs cycle in low-oxygen conditions
• Cancer's Warburg effect explained mechanistically
• Why oxygen matters for energy production
• Connection between metabolism and aging
• Origin of life through metabolism-first hypothesis
• Mitochondrial role in cell death

Why important: Deeper than most biochemistry books on the actual mechanism of energy production. Changes how you think about ketones, fasting, and cellular fuel.
Metabolism deep
📔
The Vital Question: Energy, Evolution, and the Origins of Complex Life
Author: Nick Lane

In a nutshell: Lane's third masterwork explores why life evolved the way it did, focusing on the central role of energy. Provides evolutionary context for why your metabolism works as it does.

Core concepts:
• Why energy constraints shape biology
• Why complex life requires mitochondria
• Evolution of sex and aging
• Two-cell sex differences
• Why we age and die at the cellular level
• The hyperthermal vent origin theory
• Connection between energy and consciousness

Why valuable: Together with Power, Sex, Suicide and Transformer, forms a complete trilogy on bioenergetics. Read this third.
Evolutionary biology
📔
Why We Get Sick
Author: Dr. Benjamin Bikman

In a nutshell: The definitive accessible book on insulin resistance — the root mechanism of metabolic disease. Bikman is a leading insulin researcher who makes complex science readable.

Core concepts:
• Insulin's role in fat storage vs burning
• Insulin resistance development mechanisms
• Why insulin resistance precedes type 2 diabetes by decades
• GLUT4 transporter and glucose uptake
• Insulin's effect on every cell type
• Connection between insulin and Alzheimer's (type 3 diabetes)
• Insulin-cancer connection (mTOR activation)
• PCOS, infertility, and insulin
• How to measure insulin resistance properly (HOMA-IR)
• Dietary strategies that restore insulin sensitivity

Why essential FIRST read: Single most important book for understanding why your protocol works.
Start here
📔
The Hungry Brain
Author: Dr. Stephan Guyenet

In a nutshell: Neuroscientist explains how your brain controls hunger, body weight, and food choices — often against your conscious will.

Core concepts:
• The "lipostat" — your brain's weight regulation system
• Leptin and the satiety system
• Why willpower fails at fighting hunger
• Reward systems and food palatability
• Industrial food's effect on brain wiring
• Why some foods are addictive
• Sleep and hunger hormones
• Strategies to work WITH your brain not against

Why useful for you: Explains why Mounjaro works (it modulates brain hunger circuits) and why simple caloric restriction usually fails.
Brain-hunger
📔
Tripping Over the Truth
Author: Travis Christofferson

In a nutshell: The history and science of cancer as a metabolic disease. Traces how cancer research diverged from the metabolic Warburg model to genetics, and why metabolic theory is returning.

Core concepts:
• The Warburg effect (cancer cells ferment glucose even in oxygen)
• Mitochondrial dysfunction as root cause of cancer
• Why genetic theory of cancer has fundamental limits
• Ketogenic diet's potential anti-cancer role
• Press-pulse cancer treatment strategy
• Insulin signaling and cancer growth
• Caloric restriction's anti-cancer mechanisms
• Hyperbaric oxygen and cancer

Why important: Cancer is one of the "4 horsemen." Understanding metabolic theory informs long-term protocol decisions.
Cancer mechanisms

⚗️ Hormones · endocrinology · signaling

Deep understanding of the chemical messengers that orchestrate your body. Critical for navigating TRT, understanding your blood work, and recognizing hormone-driven changes in mood, energy, and behavior.

📕
Hormonal: The Hidden Intelligence of Hormones
Author: Dr. Martie Haselton

In a nutshell: UCLA evolutionary psychologist's groundbreaking exploration of how hormones shape human behavior, attraction, decision-making, and social dynamics. Bridges endocrinology with evolutionary psychology.

Core concepts:
• Hormones as behavioral architects, not just physical regulators
• The estrus cycle and unconscious behavioral shifts
• Testosterone's role in male behavior and risk-taking
• Cortisol and stress-driven decision-making
• Oxytocin in bonding and trust
• Hormonal contraception effects on partner preference
• Sexual selection and hormonal signaling
• Why understanding hormones explains "irrational" behavior
• Evidence-based approach to hormone-behavior research

Why valuable for you: As you optimize testosterone, understanding the broader behavioral effects of hormones (not just lab numbers) helps you contextualize changes in mood, motivation, attraction, and decision-making. Important read before TRT.
Hormones & behavior
📕
Greenspan's Basic & Clinical Endocrinology
Author: David Gardner & Dolores Shoback

In a nutshell: The standard medical endocrinology reference. Covers every endocrine gland, hormone, and pathology in detail.

Core concepts:
• Hypothalamic-pituitary axis
• Thyroid physiology and pathology (relevant for your TSH 3.58)
• Adrenal hormones (cortisol, DHEA, aldosterone)
• Reproductive endocrinology (T, E2, LH, FSH)
• Growth hormone and IGF-1
• Bone metabolism and vitamin D
• Diabetes and glucose metabolism
• All endocrine feedback loops

Best used as: Look up specific endocrine topics as questions arise. Better than internet for accurate information.
Reference
📕
Williams Textbook of Endocrinology
Author: Shlomo Melmed et al.

In a nutshell: Most authoritative endocrinology textbook for clinicians. Deeper than Greenspan, used by endocrinology fellows.

Core concepts:
• Detailed hormone synthesis pathways
• Receptor biology and signaling
• Genetic and developmental endocrinology
• All endocrine disease states
• Pharmacology of hormone therapies
• Clinical decision-making frameworks
• Cutting-edge research integration

Best used as: Advanced reference. Look here when you want medical-grade depth on a specific hormone or condition.
Advanced reference
📕
The Hormone Solution
Author: Dr. Thierry Hertoghe

In a nutshell: Belgian endocrinologist's framework for multi-hormone optimization. European functional medicine perspective on testosterone, GH, thyroid, cortisol, DHEA, melatonin.

Core concepts:
• Multi-hormone optimization vs single hormone focus
• Symptom-driven hormone assessment
• Bioidentical hormone replacement
• Thyroid optimization (your TSH 3.58 case)
• Growth hormone deficiency in adults
• Cortisol rhythm restoration
• DHEA and pregnenolone basics
• Why "normal ranges" miss optimal

Why useful for you: Hertoghe practices in Brussels — accessible to you from Geneva. His framework helps find similar practitioners.
European framework
📕
Behave: The Biology of Humans at Our Best and Worst
Author: Dr. Robert Sapolsky

In a nutshell: Stanford neuroscientist's comprehensive exploration of why we behave the way we do — from neurotransmitters to hormones to genes to culture. Most acclaimed neuroscience book of last decade.

Core concepts:
• Hormonal influences on behavior (testosterone, cortisol, oxytocin)
• Stress neurobiology (Sapolsky's specialty)
• Why testosterone doesn't simply "cause" aggression
• Cortisol and chronic stress effects
• Dopamine and motivation
• Connection between brain, hormones, and behavior
• Critical thinking about hormone-behavior claims
• Why simple "X hormone causes Y behavior" is wrong

Why valuable: Complements Haselton's evolutionary psychology with neurobiological depth. Together they give you the full picture of hormones and behavior.
Neurobiology
📕
Why Zebras Don't Get Ulcers
Author: Dr. Robert Sapolsky

In a nutshell: The classic on stress physiology. How chronic stress disrupts every body system — hormonal, cardiovascular, immune, cognitive, reproductive.

Core concepts:
• Acute vs chronic stress responses
• Cortisol's destructive effects when chronically elevated
• Stress and testosterone suppression
• Stress and insulin resistance
• Stress and cardiovascular damage
• Stress and immune dysfunction
• Why humans uniquely suffer from psychological stress
• Practical stress management mechanisms

Why essential: Stress sabotages every hormonal optimization. Understanding the mechanisms transforms how you approach training, recovery, and life balance.
Stress physiology

🔬 Specific mechanisms · AMPK · mTOR · autophagy · ketosis

Deep dives into the specific cellular mechanisms that drive your protocol's effects. These books explain the molecular dance happening every moment in your body.

🔬
The Switch: Ignite Your Metabolism with Intermittent Fasting
Author: Dr. James Clement

In a nutshell: Detailed exploration of autophagy — the cellular cleanup mechanism activated by fasting. Clement interviewed 200+ centenarians and longevity researchers.

Core concepts:
• Autophagy molecular mechanisms
• The 5 senses of autophagy (mitophagy, lipophagy, etc.)
• mTOR as the master switch
• Spermidine and autophagy induction
• Centenarian biology
• Senescent cells and aging
• Practical autophagy activation strategies
• Fasting protocols for different goals

Why critical: You do 18:6 fasting daily — understand exactly what autophagy is doing in your cells during those 18 hours.
Autophagy
🔬
The Art and Science of Low Carbohydrate Living
Author: Dr. Stephen Phinney & Dr. Jeff Volek

In a nutshell: The most rigorous scientific examination of low-carb metabolism. Phinney and Volek are the researchers behind much of modern keto science.

Core concepts:
• Keto-adaptation timeline (2-12 weeks)
• Beta-hydroxybutyrate vs acetoacetate vs acetone
• Why blood ketones differ from urine/breath
• GKI (glucose-ketone index) interpretation
• Electrolyte management on keto (sodium critical)
• Athletic performance on ketosis
• Common keto mistakes
• Sustainable low-carb living

Why essential for you: You're targeting GKI <3.0 — this book explains exactly the science you need to optimize ketosis depth.
Ketosis science
🔬
The Art and Science of Low Carbohydrate Performance
Author: Dr. Stephen Phinney & Dr. Jeff Volek

In a nutshell: The sequel focusing on athletic performance during ketosis. Critical for understanding how your training and ketosis interact.

Core concepts:
• Fuel utilization during exercise (carbs vs fat)
• Maximum fat oxidation rates in keto-adapted athletes
• Why ketogenic athletes can spare glycogen
• Resistance training on keto
• Recovery considerations
• Protein requirements during keto + training
• Common performance pitfalls
• Cyclic vs continuous keto

Why critical: You train 5×/week intensely on carnivore. This book addresses your exact scenario.
Keto + training
🔬
Metabolical
Author: Dr. Robert Lustig

In a nutshell: Pediatric endocrinologist's framework for understanding how processed food drives metabolic disease. Bridges biochemistry, nutrition, and public health.

Core concepts:
• The 8 subcellular pathologies of metabolic disease
• Mitochondrial dysfunction mechanisms
• Inflammation and insulin resistance connection
• Methylation defects and metabolic disease
• Membrane lipid composition (omega-3:6 ratio)
• Why sugar specifically damages liver (like alcohol)
• Fructose's unique toxicity
• Processed food impact on hormones

Why valuable: Bridges cellular biochemistry with practical food choices. Helps you understand why food quality matters at molecular level.
Metabolic disease
🔬
The Mitochondrial Bible
Author: Dr. Lee Know

In a nutshell: Comprehensive practical guide to mitochondrial health. Combines biochemistry with actionable interventions.

Core concepts:
• Mitochondrial biogenesis triggers (cold, exercise, fasting)
• Mitophagy (selective autophagy of damaged mitochondria)
• Reactive oxygen species — when bad, when good (hormesis)
• CoQ10 and electron transport chain
• PQQ and mitochondrial biogenesis
• Mitochondrial uncoupling and metabolism
• Brown fat and UCP1
• Specific supplements for mitochondria

Why useful: Practical complement to Nick Lane's more theoretical books. Translates mitochondrial science to action.
Practical mitochondria
🔬
Ketogenic Diet and Metabolic Therapies
Author: Susan Masino (Editor)

In a nutshell: Academic textbook on the science of ketogenic metabolism. Used in medical schools. Covers therapeutic applications beyond weight loss.

Core concepts:
• Brain ketone metabolism in detail
• Ketones for epilepsy treatment (original use)
• Ketones in neurodegenerative disease
• Beta-hydroxybutyrate as signaling molecule
• HDAC inhibition by ketones
• Inflammation reduction mechanisms
• Cancer and ketosis
• Cognitive performance on ketones

Why advanced: For when you want the academic depth on ketone biology. Goes beyond Phinney/Volek for therapeutic applications.
Advanced
🔬
The Longevity Code
Author: Dr. Kris Verburgh

In a nutshell: Belgian physician's accessible framework integrating ALL the major cellular switches — AMPK, mTOR, autophagy, sirtuins, IGF-1. Best single book covering these pathways together.

Core concepts:
• AMPK as cellular energy sensor (activated by low ATP)
• mTOR as growth/anabolic sensor (activated by amino acids/insulin)
• The AMPK-mTOR opposition (you can't be in both states)
• Autophagy as cellular cleanup
• Sirtuins and NAD+ in longevity
• IGF-1 and the protein-longevity paradox
• Hormesis (good stress activating these switches)
• How fasting, exercise, cold all converge on these pathways
• Practical interventions to activate longevity switches

Why critical for your question: This is the single best accessible book that covers AMPK comprehensively alongside mTOR, autophagy, and growth hormone interactions.
AMPK + mTOR + autophagy
🔬
The Hallmarks of Aging (academic paper)
Authors: López-Otín, Blasco, Partridge, Serrano, Kroemer

In a nutshell: Not a book — the most influential academic paper on aging biology (2013, updated 2023). Free online. Establishes the unified framework of 12 hallmarks including deregulated nutrient sensing.

Core concepts:
• The 12 hallmarks of aging framework
• Deregulated nutrient sensing (AMPK, mTOR, insulin, IGF-1, sirtuins)
• Mitochondrial dysfunction in aging
• Cellular senescence mechanisms
• Stem cell exhaustion
• Loss of proteostasis
• Telomere attrition
• Epigenetic alterations
• Genomic instability
• Inflammaging
• Altered intercellular communication
• How these hallmarks interconnect

Where to read: Search "Hallmarks of Aging" on PubMed/Google Scholar. Free PDF available. Most-cited aging paper ever published.

Why read this BEFORE aging books: Gives you the unified framework all longevity books reference.
Free academic paper
🔬
Growth Hormone in Health and Disease
Author: Multiple academic editors

In a nutshell: Academic medical reference on growth hormone physiology, deficiency, therapy, and controversies. The deepest single source on GH.

Core concepts:
• Pulsatile GH secretion (3-5 pulses/day)
• Sleep-dependent GH release (deep sleep critical)
• IGF-1 as downstream messenger
• Somatopause (age-related GH decline)
• Exercise-induced GH spikes
• GH and insulin opposing effects
• GH replacement therapy protocols
• The longevity-anabolic paradox
• Cancer risks of elevated IGF-1
• Why high-quality sleep maximizes natural GH

Best used as: Reference for understanding GH science deeply. More accessible than Williams Endocrinology specifically for GH.
GH reference
🔬
Lifespan
Author: Dr. David Sinclair

In a nutshell: Harvard biologist's framework for aging as a treatable disease. Covers sirtuins, NAD+, AMPK, mTOR, and the genetics of longevity.

Core concepts (relevant to your AMPK question):
• Information theory of aging
• Sirtuins as "longevity genes" (activated by NAD+)
• AMPK activation by exercise, fasting, metformin
• mTOR inhibition for longevity (rapamycin)
• The IGF-1 longevity paradox
• Hormesis: good stress activates longevity genes
• Why caloric restriction extends life (AMPK/sirtuin activation)
• Cold exposure and longevity gene activation
• Emerging interventions (NMN, NR, metformin)
• Epigenetic age reversal possibilities

Why useful for AMPK specifically: Sinclair gives clearer mechanistic explanation of AMPK activation than most popular books.
AMPK + sirtuins

🧠 Cortisol · HPA axis · chronic stress

The stress system that, when chronically activated, sabotages every other optimization — testosterone, insulin, sleep, mood, body composition. Critical to understand and manage.

🧠
Why Zebras Don't Get Ulcers
Author: Dr. Robert Sapolsky

In a nutshell: The single best book on stress physiology — already in your library but worth emphasizing here. Stanford neuroendocrinologist explains why humans are uniquely vulnerable to chronic stress.

Core concepts (cortisol/HPA-specific):
• The HPA axis: Hypothalamus → Pituitary → Adrenal cascade
• CRH → ACTH → Cortisol signaling chain
• Diurnal cortisol rhythm (peak at waking, low at sleep)
• Acute vs chronic cortisol responses
• Glucocorticoid receptor downregulation
• Cortisol suppression of testosterone (HPG axis)
• Cortisol and insulin resistance development
• Cortisol effects on hippocampus and memory
• Why "fight or flight" becomes "freeze and persist" in chronic stress
• Cortisol and immune dysfunction
• Practical stress management at molecular level

Why essential for you: Your protocol's hormone optimization requires healthy cortisol rhythm. Ashwagandha addresses this but understanding the mechanism deepens the intervention.
Cortisol essential
🧠
Adrenal Fatigue: The 21st Century Stress Syndrome
Author: Dr. James Wilson

In a nutshell: Practical guide to recognizing and addressing HPA axis dysfunction. Controversial in mainstream medicine but useful framework for the symptom cluster.

Core concepts:
• Stages of HPA axis dysfunction
• Salivary cortisol testing patterns
• Why morning cortisol matters most
• Reverse cortisol rhythm (high at night) implications
• DHEA:cortisol ratio
• Adaptogens and HPA support
• Lifestyle factors in HPA recovery
• Why "adrenal fatigue" terminology is debated but symptoms are real

Why useful with caveat: Read critically — "adrenal fatigue" as discrete diagnosis is contested. But the underlying HPA dysregulation concept is real and the framework is practical.
Practical HPA
🧠
The End of Stress as We Know It
Author: Dr. Bruce McEwen & Elizabeth Lasley

In a nutshell: McEwen coined the term "allostatic load" — the cumulative wear and tear of chronic stress. Most influential framework for understanding how stress damages the body over time.

Core concepts:
• Allostasis vs homeostasis
• Allostatic load and accumulated damage
• The four types of allostatic load
• Cortisol's brain effects (hippocampus, amygdala, prefrontal cortex)
• Stress and the cardiovascular system
• Stress and metabolic disease
• Resilience factors
• Practical interventions backed by neuroscience

Why valuable: Provides the academic framework that Sapolsky popularized. Together they give comprehensive understanding.
Academic framework

🦋 Thyroid hormone · T3 · T4 · metabolism control

The master regulator of metabolic rate. Your TSH at 3.58 mIU/L is upper-normal range — understanding thyroid deeply matters for optimizing your metabolism.

🦋
Why Do I Still Have Thyroid Symptoms?
Author: Dr. Datis Kharrazian

In a nutshell: Functional medicine framework for thyroid dysfunction beyond simple hypo/hyper diagnoses. Best practical book on real-world thyroid issues.

Core concepts:
• T4 → T3 conversion (where most people fail)
• Why TSH alone misses thyroid problems
• Reverse T3 and stress effects
• Hashimoto's autoimmunity (90% of "hypothyroid")
• Selenium's role in T4→T3 conversion
• Iodine controversy (too little vs too much)
• Thyroid hormone receptor resistance
• Why your TSH 3.58 isn't necessarily "normal"
• Functional vs lab-range interpretation
• Practical lifestyle interventions

Why critical for you: Your TSH 3.58 is upper-normal — Kharrazian's framework explains why this might need attention, especially with your selenium supplementation already in place.
Thyroid essential
🦋
Stop the Thyroid Madness
Author: Janie Bowthorpe

In a nutshell: Patient-advocate's comprehensive guide to thyroid optimization. Started a movement among hypothyroid patients dissatisfied with synthetic T4-only treatment.

Core concepts:
• Why T4-only therapy (Levothyroxine) fails many patients
• Desiccated thyroid hormone (NDT) options
• T3 supplementation rationale
• Comprehensive thyroid panel beyond TSH
• Free T3, Free T4, Reverse T3
• Thyroid antibodies (TPO, TgAb)
• Iron, ferritin, B12, vitamin D connections
• Adrenal-thyroid relationship
• Patient advocacy framework

Why useful: Patient-centered perspective complements Kharrazian's clinical framework. Important if you ever need thyroid replacement.
Patient perspective
🦋
The Thyroid Connection
Author: Dr. Amy Myers

In a nutshell: Functional medicine MD's framework for resolving thyroid dysfunction through addressing root causes — gut, infection, stress, toxins.

Core concepts:
• Gut-thyroid axis
• Leaky gut and autoimmunity
• Hidden infections affecting thyroid
• Toxin exposure (mercury, fluoride)
• Diet interventions for thyroid (controversial — pro-paleo)
• Stress and thyroid suppression
• Practical protocols

Why useful with caveat: Some claims oversimplified. Read with critical lens but the gut-thyroid connection is real.
Root cause approach

🦠 Gut microbiome · gut-brain axis · digestive physiology

The 100 trillion bacteria in your gut affect every aspect of health — metabolism, immunity, hormones, mood, cognition. Critical to understand on carnivore diet specifically.

🦠
The Mind-Gut Connection
Author: Dr. Emeran Mayer

In a nutshell: UCLA gastroenterologist's exploration of the bidirectional communication between gut and brain. Best accessible book on the gut-brain axis.

Core concepts:
• The enteric nervous system ("second brain")
• Vagus nerve as gut-brain highway
• Gut bacteria producing neurotransmitters (90% of serotonin made in gut)
• How gut microbiome affects mood
• Gut feelings as real biology, not metaphor
• Stress effects on gut function
• Microbiome and decision-making
• Why intermittent fasting helps gut
• Diversity of microbiome and health
• Diet's rapid effect on microbiome composition

Why valuable: Foundation for understanding gut's role beyond digestion. Carnivore diet drastically changes microbiome — understanding the mechanism is important.
Gut-brain essential
🦠
10% Human
Author: Dr. Alanna Collen

In a nutshell: Evolutionary biologist's exploration of how the microbiome shaped human evolution and health. Why we're 90% bacteria by cell count.

Core concepts:
• Coevolution of humans and microbiome
• Birth method (vaginal vs C-section) microbiome effects
• Antibiotics as ecosystem destruction
• Hygiene hypothesis
• Modern lifestyle's microbiome devastation
• Microbiome and obesity
• Microbiome and autoimmune disease
• Why diverse microbiome correlates with health

Why important: Provides historical/evolutionary context for current gut health crisis. Helps you understand why preserving microbiome matters.
Evolutionary context
🦠
Fiber Fueled
Author: Dr. Will Bulsiewicz

In a nutshell: Gastroenterologist's case for plant-fiber-based gut health. Important counter-perspective to carnivore approach — read critically.

Core concepts:
• Microbiome diversity arguments
• Short-chain fatty acids (butyrate, propionate, acetate)
• Fiber fermentation by gut bacteria
• Plant diversity studies
• Carnivore diet criticism perspective
• Mediterranean diet evidence
• Polyphenols and microbiome

Why read with critical lens: Strong anti-carnivore bias. But understanding the opposing position makes you smarter. Cross-reference with Saladino's Carnivore Code for both sides.

Honest take: Both Bulsiewicz and Saladino have valid points and biases. Truth is probably in the middle for most people, but n=1 experimentation matters.
Counter-perspective
🦠
Gut: The Inside Story of Our Body's Most Underrated Organ
Author: Dr. Giulia Enders

In a nutshell: German physician's accessible, often humorous tour of digestive physiology. Best entry-level book on how your gut actually works.

Core concepts:
• Digestive process from mouth to anus
• The ileocecal valve and bacterial migration
• Gut wall structure and absorption
• Allergies and intolerances at gut level
• Probiotics: hype vs reality
• Constipation physiology
• Bowel movements as health indicators
• Cultural attitudes toward gut health

Why useful: Foundational understanding of digestion before reading deeper microbiome books. Practical and engaging.

Chronic inflammation is the common denominator of nearly all modern disease — cardiovascular, metabolic, autoimmune, neurodegenerative, cancer. Understanding it lets you address root causes.

🔥
Inflamed: Deep Medicine and the Anatomy of Injustice
Author: Dr. Rupa Marya & Raj Patel

In a nutshell: Physician and food expert connect inflammation to social, environmental, and dietary factors. Broader perspective than typical medical books.

Core concepts:
• Inflammation as protective vs pathological
• Why acute inflammation heals but chronic destroys
• Connection between social stress and inflammation
• Food system's inflammatory impact
• Environmental toxins and immune dysfunction
• Inflammaging (inflammation in aging)
• Indigenous medicine perspectives
• Inflammation across body systems

Why valuable: Broader perspective than purely biochemical inflammation books. Connects micro and macro levels.
Inflammation big picture
🔥
The Inflammation Spectrum

Core concepts:
• Five types of chronic inflammation
• Inflammation symptom mapping
• Elimination diet protocols
• Reintroduction testing
• Personal inflammatory triggers
• Gut-inflammation connection
• Stress-inflammation connection
• Sleep-inflammation connection

Why practical: Actionable framework with specific protocols. Carnivore is essentially the ultimate elimination diet — this book explains why and how to reintroduce foods strategically.
Practical protocol

⚡ Neurotransmitters · dopamine · serotonin · motivation

The chemical messengers that drive motivation, mood, focus, and behavior. Understanding them helps you optimize protocol adherence, training intensity, and life satisfaction.

⚡
The Molecule of More
Author: Daniel Z. Lieberman & Michael E. Long

In a nutshell: Best accessible book on dopamine. Reframes dopamine as the "anticipation/pursuit" chemical, not the "pleasure" chemical (common misconception).

Core concepts:
• Dopamine drives wanting, not liking
• The dopamine-vs-here-and-now neurochemistry distinction
• Why anticipation feels better than achievement
• Dopamine in addiction (drugs, food, social media, porn)
• Dopamine and creativity
• Why "achievement disease" never satisfies
• Practical dopamine management
• Cold exposure and dopamine spike
• Why fasting feels rewarding
• Sustainable vs unsustainable dopamine sources

Why critical for your protocol: Cold plunges, fasting, training all dramatically affect dopamine. Understanding this helps you sustain protocol motivation long-term.
Dopamine essential
⚡
Dopamine Nation
Author: Dr. Anna Lembke

In a nutshell: Stanford psychiatrist's framework for understanding and managing dopamine in age of abundance. Practical addiction recovery applied to all overconsumption.

Core concepts:
• The pleasure-pain balance (homeostasis)
• Why pursuing pleasure causes pain
• Dopamine fasting concept
• Modern life as dopamine overload
• Why discomfort intentionally improves wellbeing
• Cold, exercise, fasting as healthy dopamine triggers
• Addiction mechanisms applied broadly
• Practical interventions

Why valuable for your protocol: Your protocol is essentially a dopamine reset — cold, fasting, training all create healthy dopamine patterns. This book explains why these feel difficult initially but rewarding long-term.
Dopamine practice
⚡
Drive: The Surprising Truth About What Motivates Us
Author: Daniel Pink

In a nutshell: Pink synthesizes motivation research into three core drivers: autonomy, mastery, purpose. Less neurobiology, more practical application.

Core concepts:
• Intrinsic vs extrinsic motivation
• Why rewards often decrease motivation
• Autonomy as fundamental need
• Mastery and the flow state
• Purpose as motivational fuel
• Why deadlines work and rewards don't
• Flow states and dopamine
• Application to life design

Why useful for your protocol: 18-month protocol requires sustained motivation. Pink's framework explains why intrinsic motivation (mastery, autonomy, purpose) beats willpower.
Motivation science
⚡
Spark: The Revolutionary New Science of Exercise and the Brain
Author: Dr. John Ratey

In a nutshell: Harvard psychiatrist's exploration of how exercise specifically affects brain chemistry — dopamine, serotonin, norepinephrine, BDNF.

Core concepts:
• BDNF (brain-derived neurotrophic factor)
• Exercise as "Miracle-Gro for the brain"
• Exercise and depression
• Exercise and anxiety mechanisms
• Exercise and ADHD
• Cardio vs strength for brain benefits
• Neurogenesis (new brain cell growth)
• Why exercise rivals medications for mood

Why valuable: Your 5×/week training has profound brain benefits beyond muscle. This book explains the neurochemistry.
Exercise + brain

🧠 Mental performance · discipline · habit

📚
Can't Hurt Me
Author: David Goggins

In a nutshell: Former Navy SEAL's brutal autobiography and mental framework for breaking past self-imposed limitations.

Core concepts:
• The 40% rule (you're capable of 40% more than you think)
• Accountability mirror practice
• Suffering as growth catalyst
• Mental calluses through deliberate discomfort
• The cookie jar mental technique
• Visualization for hardship
• Why comfort is the enemy
• Identity through proven action

Why valuable: Your protocol requires sustained discomfort (cold plunges, fasting, training, dietary restriction). Goggins provides the mental framework for embracing rather than fighting that discomfort.
Mental toughness
📚
Atomic Habits
Author: James Clear

In a nutshell: Practical framework for building sustainable habits through small, compound changes. Most actionable habit-formation book available.

Core concepts:
• The four laws of behavior change
• Habit stacking technique
• Environment design over willpower
• Identity-based habits ("I am someone who...")
• The 1% improvement principle
• The plateau of latent potential
• Why systems beat goals
• Making habits obvious, attractive, easy, satisfying

Why critical: Your 18-month transformation requires habit sustainability. This book provides the mechanism for making your protocol elements automatic rather than effortful.
Habit formation
📚
Deep Work
Author: Cal Newport

In a nutshell: Framework for focused, undistracted work that creates value. Relevant for protocol design and life optimization beyond physical transformation.

Core concepts:
• The economic value of deep work
• Attention residue and task-switching cost
• Four philosophies for scheduling deep work
• Boredom training (resisting constant distraction)
• The shallow work trap
• Rituals for productive focus
• Why social media degrades attention

Why useful: Mental clarity supports protocol adherence. Cognitive performance optimization complements physical optimization.
Focus

⏳ Longevity · aging · long-term optimization

📖
Lifespan
Author: Dr. David Sinclair

In a nutshell: Harvard biologist's framework for understanding aging as a treatable disease. Covers sirtuins, NAD+, hormesis, and emerging anti-aging interventions.

Core concepts:
• The information theory of aging
• Sirtuins and the "longevity genes"
• NAD+ decline with age
• Hormesis (good stress for longevity)
• Why fasting extends life
• Cold exposure and longevity
• Exercise and senescent cell clearance
• Emerging interventions (metformin, rapamycin, NMN)
• Epigenetic age reversal

Why important: Provides scientific framework for why your protocol elements (fasting, cold, exercise) extend healthspan, not just lifespan.
Longevity science
📖
The Longevity Diet
Author: Dr. Valter Longo

In a nutshell: USC researcher's framework based on studying centenarian populations and fasting science.

Core concepts:
• The Fasting Mimicking Diet (FMD)
• Why protein restriction matters in some phases
• IGF-1 and aging
• The Blue Zones research nuances
• Mediterranean diet patterns
• mTOR and longevity
• Plant-forward but flexible approach

Why useful: Counterpoint to pure carnivore — helps you understand the protein/longevity debate and informs long-term maintenance phase decisions.
Longevity nutrition
📖
The Telomere Effect
Author: Dr. Elizabeth Blackburn & Dr. Elissa Epel

In a nutshell: Nobel Prize-winning research on telomeres — the protective caps on chromosomes that shorten with age. Practical implications for lifestyle.

Core concepts:
• Telomere biology basics
• Stress and telomere shortening
• Exercise and telomere length
• Diet and telomere health
• Sleep and cellular aging
• Relationships and longevity
• Meditation and telomeres

Why important: Connects lifestyle factors directly to cellular aging mechanisms.
Cellular aging

📝 How to learn effectively from these resources

⏱️
Time investment
Most books: 8-15 hours total
Audiobook option: listen during commute or training
30 min/day × 6 weeks = book completed

Recommended pace:
1 book per month = 12-15 books/year
By end of 18-month protocol: deep expertise in your own health

Don't try to read everything fast. Slow integration beats speed.
Pacing
📓
Active reading method
For each book:
1. Read the introduction + table of contents first
2. Read each chapter, then summarize in 3-5 sentences
3. Note any protocol modifications it suggests
4. Apply ONE new insight before next chapter
5. Re-visit key chapters quarterly

Passive reading wastes time. Active integration changes behavior.
Method
🎯
Critical thinking
Even authors I recommend have:
• Biases (commercial, professional, ideological)
• Outdated information
• Errors and overstatements
• Areas outside their expertise

Cross-reference:
When one book makes a strong claim, check if 2-3 others confirm it
Look for primary research (PubMed)
Be skeptical of universal solutions

Build your own model, not someone else's.
Critical thinking
🎯 LIFELONG LEARNING COMMITMENT: This library is not about reading more — it's about building the deep understanding that lets you make better decisions for the next 30+ years. Your protocol will evolve as you learn. The investment in knowledge compounds over time, becoming the most valuable asset for your health. Start with one book this month. By Month 6 you'll have a different relationship with your body. By Year 2, you'll be teaching others. 📚

❄️ Cold exposure variation protocol — phased approach

📚 PRINCIPLE (Huberman-inspired): Variation prevents over-adaptation. Like training, identical cold stimulus eventually loses effectiveness. Norepinephrine response decreases 30-50% with repetitive identical sessions. Variation in temperature, duration, time of day, and approach maintains the stress response and continued adaptation. Same principle as progressive overload in resistance training.
1️⃣
Phase 1 — Foundation
Duration: Days 1-14 (3-17 June 2026)

Protocol:
• 4-5 sessions/week
• 12-13°C consistently
• 3 minutes each
• Morning fasted preferred (07:00)

Focus: Build base tolerance · establish consistency · track recovery patterns

You are HERE. Day 2 of 14.
Current phase
2️⃣
Phase 2 — Light variation
Duration: Days 15-28 (18 June - 1 July)

Protocol:
• 3 sessions at 12-13°C / 3 min
• 1 session/week colder: 10-11°C / 2 min
• 1 session/week longer: 12°C / 4 min

Focus: Introduce variation gradually · monitor response · adjust based on recovery

Body learns multiple stimuli without full adaptation.
Next phase
3️⃣
Phase 3 — Full variation
Duration: Days 29-56 (2-29 July)

4-type rotation per week:
• 2× Standard (12°C / 3 min)
• 1× Cold shock (8-10°C / 90 sec)
• 1× Endurance (14-15°C / 5 min)
• 1× Contrast Saturday (sauna+cold rounds)

Focus: Maximum adaptation · prevent plateaus · build resilience
July phase
4️⃣
Phase 4 — Advanced
Duration: Days 57+ (August onward)

Protocol:
• Continue 4-type rotation
• Push intensity gradually
• Cold shock down to 6-8°C
• Endurance up to 6-7 min
• Maintain weekly variety

Focus: Long-term maintenance · advanced adaptation · lifestyle integration
Long-term

🔥 Sauna protocol — confirmed strategy

✅ DECISION 6 June 2026: KEEP sauna 45 min × 5 training days post-workout, BUT use the time productively (podcasts, books, learning). PLUS add contrast thermal therapy on rest days (2×/week) — 20 min sauna alternated with cold plunge, 2-3 rounds, ending COLD. This delivers full sauna benefits + Saturday/Sunday contrast variation (Huberman principle) for maximum hormonal response.
🏋️
Training days (5×/week)
Post-workout sauna 45 min

Productive use of time:
• Audiobook (1 chapter of bible resources)
• Huberman/Attia podcast
• Phone calls (family)
• Meditation or breathwork
• Note review (refine protocol)

Benefits stack: HSP activation + cardiovascular adaptation + knowledge building

Hydration during: 500ml water + pinch salt
Daily routine
❄️🔥
Rest days (2×/week)
Contrast thermal therapy

Protocol:
• Round 1: 20 min sauna → 2-3 min cold plunge
• Round 2: 15-20 min sauna → 2-3 min cold plunge
• Round 3: 15-20 min sauna → 2-3 min cold plunge

Total time: 60-90 min

Critical rule: ALWAYS END COLD (vasoconstriction = anti-inflammatory)

Hormonal effect: Maximum norepinephrine cascades from transitions
Contrast days
📊
Weekly totals
Heat exposure:
• 5 × 45 min training day sauna = 225 min
• 2 × 60-80 min contrast (sauna portion) = 80-120 min
• Total: ~305-345 min/week

Cold exposure:
• 3-4 standard cold plunges (3 min) = ~12 min
• 2 contrast sessions (3 rounds × 3 min) = ~18 min
• Total: ~30 min/week

Far exceeds Huberman's 11 min/week target for cold (good — your protocol is more aggressive).
Weekly volume

🔥❄️ Contrast thermal therapy — rest day protocol details

RoundSauna phaseCold plungePauseNotes
Pre-session———Hydrate 500ml water + salt · empty bladder
Round 120 min @ max temp2-3 min @ 12°C30-60 secBody acclimates to heat first
Round 215-20 min2-3 min @ 12°C30-60 secSweat response peaks here
Round 315-20 min2-3 min @ 12°C—🚨 END COLD - no sauna after final plunge
Post-session———500ml water + salt · 10 min gentle movement to rewarming

Total session: 60-90 min · Best timing: Saturday morning or Sunday morning · Use rest days when no resistance training stress

🎯 WHY END COLD IS CRITICAL: Research by Dr. Susanna Søberg established that ending hot (sauna) causes continued vasodilation and inflammation, while ending cold causes vasoconstriction and inflammation reduction. The order of finishing determines whether you get inflammation amplification or reduction. ALWAYS end with cold plunge, never reverse.
🧬
Why contrast > either alone
Vascular reactivity training:
• Vessels learn to dilate then constrict efficiently
• Improves circulation long-term
• Cardiovascular adaptation accelerated

Hormonal cascade:
• Each transition spikes norepinephrine
• 3 rounds = 6 hormonal events vs 1
• Maximum BAT activation
• Sustained dopamine elevation post-session

Mental resilience:
• Each transition is a fresh challenge
• Builds psychological adaptability
• Different from routine cold or routine sauna
Why contrast
📅
Weekly placement
Best days for contrast:
• Saturday morning (after week of training)
• Sunday morning (before new week)

Why rest days:
• Body not already managing training stress
• Maximum recovery resources available
• Hormonal response amplified
• Mental space to engage with intensity

Avoid contrast on:
• Heavy training days (stress overload)
• Mounjaro injection days (additional stress)
• Days with poor sleep (no buffer)
• Evening times (sleep disruption)
Schedule
⚠️
Safety considerations
Hydration critical:
• 500ml water + salt before
• Optional 250ml between rounds
• 500ml water + salt after

Watch for:
• Lightheadedness on standing
• Severe shivering that doesn't stop
• Chest discomfort
• Heart rhythm changes
• Inability to warm hands/feet within 30 min

If any warning sign: stop session, hydrate, rest
Safety

Protocol: 45 min post-training, 5×/week · Traditional 80–90°C OR infrared 55–65°C · HSP70 activation peaks at 30+ min ≥80°C · BDNF spike requires 4×/week minimum

🌡️
What to track per session
Required:
• Date and time of session
• Type (traditional / infrared / steam)
• Temperature (°C)
• Duration (minutes)
• Timing relative to training

Useful:
• Heart rate during (target: 100-150 bpm)
• Number of "rounds" if contrast therapy
• Hydration before/after (L water)
• Electrolyte intake post-session
• Subjective fatigue/recovery rating (1-10)
Session data
📈
Why track it longitudinally
Sauna delivers measurable cumulative benefits that tracking reveals:

• HSP70 production compounds with frequency
• Cardiovascular adaptation (lower resting HR over months)
• Heat tolerance increases (longer duration possible)
• Cortisol management (chronic stress reduction)
• BDNF elevation at 4+ sessions/week
• Growth hormone response (post-session spike)

Tracking lets you correlate sauna consistency with weight loss, sleep quality, recovery, and mood.
Cumulative effects
⚖️
Contrast therapy specifics
When combining sauna + ice bath (your contrast Saturday routine), track:

• Order (sauna→cold or cold→sauna)
• Number of rounds
• Rest between transitions
• Total session time
• End state (hot or cold)

Optimal protocol: Always end COLD for inflammation reduction. Standard: 3 rounds of 15 min sauna + 2-3 min ice bath, ending with cold.

🚨 SUCRALOSE AUDIT — 4 June 2026: Three sucralose sources identified: (1) Vit4ever electrolyte powder ~80-160mg/day [ELIMINATED ✓], (2) Canderel liquid sucralose in coffee ~17-34mg/day [maintained], (3) Flavored creatine powder (blackcurrant) ~30-60mg/day [maintained]. Decision: Phased elimination — test Vit4ever removal alone first. Total daily exposure dropped from ~127-254mg to ~47-94mg (~60-70% reduction). This is the largest single change without disrupting other routines.
🧪 PHASED ELIMINATION TEST: Week 1 (4-11 June 2026): Vit4ever removed · Canderel + flavored creatine maintained · Measure GKI daily before cold plunge. Decision tree Wednesday 11 June: If GKI consistently <3.0 → Vit4ever was the bottleneck, current setup is sufficient long-term. If GKI 3.0-4.0 → significant progress, optional next step: switch to unflavored creatine. If GKI >4.0 → eliminate Canderel and/or creatine for next test phase, also investigate other variables (hidden carbs in seasonings, protein excess, sleep quality, stress).
💡 KEY SYNERGIES: Magnesium + Glycine + L-Theanine + Ashwagandha taken together create a powerful sleep stack — each works differently (temp drop, alpha waves, GABA, cortisol drop) for compound effect. D3 + K2 must always be together (K2 directs calcium). Boron + Zinc both lower SHBG (raise free T) — taken together at first meal for synergy. Omega-3 absorption requires dietary fat, which carnivore meals abundantly provide.

⚠️ Timing rules — never break these

RuleWhyConsequence if broken
Berberine NOT within 2h of trainingAMPK directly inhibits mTOR30-50% muscle protein synthesis loss
D3 ALWAYS with K2K2 directs Ca to bones, not arteriesArterial calcification risk over months
Fat-soluble vitamins WITH foodNeed dietary fat for absorption (D, K, E, A, omega-3)Wasted supplementation, no benefit
Ashwagandha at NIGHT not morningLowers cortisol — needed low at night, high in morningDaytime fatigue, sleep dysregulation
Zinc AWAY from calciumCompete for absorption sitesReduced zinc uptake = reduced T support
Morning fasted = MINIMAL supplementsEmpty stomach + Mounjaro = GI distressNausea, cramping, poor absorption
L-Carnitine BEFORE trainingMobilizes FFA when you need them mostWasted dose if taken post-training

How to use this dashboard

📲
Share data naturally
Just tell me what you measured, in any format:

• "Today weight 105.5"
• "Glucose 88, ketones 0.6"
• "BP 125/82 this morning"
• "Injection 4 done, 5mg, Friday"
• "Waist 102, hips 110"

I'll confirm, compute derived metrics, and update this dashboard.
Just share
📥
Download updated version
Each time I update the dashboard, I'll generate a fresh bible file. Download it and replace your current version.

This becomes the canonical record — the data persists across our conversations because it lives in your file, not just my memory.

Recommend: keep dated backups (e.g., bible_2026-06-03.html, bible_2026-06-10.html) so you have version history.
Replace each update
📈
Review trends regularly
Every 4 weeks, take 10 minutes to:

• Look at trajectory tables
• Note what's working vs not
• Identify any concerning trends
• Adjust protocol if needed

The longitudinal view reveals patterns invisible in daily measurements. Trust the trend, not the single data point.
